Panel title/empty copy updated. studio-pulse comparator unaffected (last-row-before-month-start now = month-end snapshot, still honest). |

| 424 | MIA-BOOKCHECK.1 — Glofox booking success judged on the created booking id, not HTTP status | Glofox `POST /2.0/bookings` returned HTTP 200 with `{ message_code: 'YOU_HAVE_NO_CREDITS_LEFT' }` (Lucinda Kinghan 2026-07-27 ×2, Colm Keegan 2026-07-22); Mia's auto-mode `book_class` judged on `result.ok` alone, marked the audit row `actioned`, and told the customer they were booked. New `interpretBookingResult()` in `src/lib/glofox.js` (success = HTTP ok + harvested `id`/`_id`/`booking_id`/`data.id`/`data._id`; failure surfaces `message_code`) now judges all three createBooking consumers: agent auto path (`booking-tools.js` — finalises `failed`, returns `{ booked:false, reason }`), approval execution (`membership-requests/[id]` — no confirmation send on a failed body), and the /start-funnel processor (already-booked re-run exemption kept). Success paths now persist `glofox_booking_id` in `details.result` for reconciliation. Event bookings untouched (internal `registerSoloEventEntry`, not Glofox). Tests: `interpretBookingResult` unit suite, 200-failure-body regressions in booking-tools-audit + processor, and a new route test for the approval branch. |
| 423 | REVIEW-LOGIN-HARDEN.1 (DB layer) — per-IP limiter + staff de-grant for the App Store reviewer login | DB half of champ-app PR #90 (which env-gates the reviewer gate code and calls a DB-backed throttle before the credential check). **Mig 449:** `review_login_attempts` (RLS enabled, no policies — service-role only) + `review_login_rate_ok(p_ip)` SECURITY DEFINER fn (search_path pinned, EXECUTE revoked from anon/authenticated): records every attempt, allows ≤10 per IP per trailing 15 min, prunes rows older than a day on each call. **Data fix:** the `appreview@un1tdublin.com` demo account had a `profiles` row + `profile_locations` role `staff` (mig-404 auto-mint era), so a session minted by the public reviewer route carried CRM staff access — both rows deleted; the member-side `contacts` row (seeded demo data) is untouched. Operational: `REVIEW_LOGIN_CODE` must be set (new, rotated value) in champ-app's serving Vercel envs or the route 404s (intended fail-closed). |
| 422 | STUDIO-KIOSK.FIX — kiosk no longer signs staff out everywhere or receives their notifications | Two operator-reported kiosk quirks, one root cause each. **(1) Cross-device logout:** every `supabase.auth.signOut()` in the estate ran unscoped — supabase-js defaults to `scope:'global'`, which revokes EVERY refresh token the user holds. On a paired native studio device the 5-min idle lock (`StudioPinProvider.lock()` → auth-context `signOut`) therefore signed the last-unlocked staffer out of their own phone and web sessions; same class fires from the Mac shell (whose WKWebView persists a full Supabase session across launches — `desktop/src-tauri/src/main.rs`) and from any web sign-out. Fix: `{ scope: 'local' }` on all six call sites — `mobile/lib/auth-context.jsx`, `Sidebar.jsx`, `PlatformShell.jsx`, `AccountShell.jsx`, `HostSignOut.jsx` — kiosk-minted sessions still get revoked server-side (local scope kills the calling session), personal sign-outs become per-device as users expect. **(2) Kiosk push notifications:** `(tabs)/_layout.jsx` registered the device push token for whoever's profile loaded, with no pairing check — so each PIN unlock upserted the kiosk's Expo token under that staffer and the shared gym device received their lead/WhatsApp alerts (customer PII). Fix: `registerForPushNotifications()` now returns `{skipped, reason:'studio_device'}` when `getPairing()` is truthy, checked BEFORE the iOS permission prompt so kiosks never even ask. Existing stale kiosk rows in `device_tokens` self-heal: the next lock's `unregisterCurrentDevicePush()` still deletes by token, and re-registration is now blocked. Tests: 2 new in `push-register.test.js` (paired ⇒ skip with no permission/token/API call; unpaired ⇒ registers). No schema/route changes. Full CI mirror green (9,025 tests). |
| 421 | BATHROOM-CLIMATE.3 — class-driven bathroom AC schedule (new `bathroom_climate` automation) | New automation, deliberately separate from the existing gym-floor `class_climate` (different timing model, own fire-log key, no shared config). **Pure planner** `src/lib/bathroom-climate.js`: bathroom units go ON a fixed delay AFTER a class STARTS (default 45 min — when people hit the showers, not class end) and run for a fixed timer (default 30 min), both operator-configurable per config blob (`delay_after_start_min`, `run_duration_min`, plus `device_ids`/`class_filter`/`excluded_slots` reusing the `class-climate` slot/filter helpers); `resolveConfig()` coerces a hand-edited JSONB so a bad value can't crash the runner, floors `run_duration_min` at 1. `planBathroomClimate()` fires on a closed `[start+delay, start+delay+duration]` window (both-sided, so a cron catching up after downtime doesn't blast stale ONs); `autoOffAtFor()` anchors the off-time to the class schedule itself (start+delay+duration, clamped to now+60s) so a late cron tick still switches off at the right wall-clock time — the OFF is never planned by this automation directly, it hands `auto_off_at` to the existing `ac-auto-off` cron. **Runner** `src/lib/bathroom-climate-runner.js`: `runBathroomClimate(db, { locationId, dryRun })` pulls occurrences from a 2h lookback, skips cancelled classes, dispatches through the existing ac-devices dispatcher, writes `automation_fire_log` rows keyed `automation_key='bathroom_climate'`, and skip/fail paths mirror `class_climate`'s runner shape. **Cron + heartbeat**: `src/app/api/cron/bathroom-climate/route.js` on `*/5` (`vercel.json`), migration 447 adds its `cron_heartbeats` row so the new schedule doesn't read as stale/unknown from the first tick; `stampHeartbeat()` on success. **Dispatch wiring**: registry entry in `src/lib/automations/registry.js` plus `bathroom_climate` branches added to both `/api/automations/[key]/run-now` and `/api/automations/[key]/schedule` (both routes had gaps for a non-`class_climate` key, closed here). **UI**: new `BathroomClimateCard.jsx` on `/automations` — device picker (bathroom ThinQ units), the two delay/duration fields, class-name filter, excluded-slots list, Preview-only (dry-run) against the live timetable, and the enable/save toggle; `check:mobile-parity` passes untouched since `automations` is already a web-only-by-design excluded key, so no new `WEB_PERMISSIONS` entry was needed. Tests: 24 (`bathroom-climate.test.js` + `bathroom-climate-runner.test.js`) covering config coercion/floors, window open/close boundaries, filter/exclusion matching, `autoOffAtFor` anchoring + clamp, and the runner's lookback/cancelled-skip/dispatch/fire-log/error paths. Full local CI mirror green (9021 tests, lint 0 errors, mobile-parity/mobile-imports/route-guards/guardrails all clean). Migration 447 NOT yet applied — orchestrator applies via Supabase MCP before merge (see PR ship-time checklist). |
| 420 | CONTRACTS-PDF.1 — signed PDF artifact (generate, store, attach, download) | Closes the "PDF generation is deliberately deferred" stub in `src/app/api/contracts/[id]/sign/route.js`. **Library:** `@react-pdf/renderer` ^4.5.1 added to the ROOT `package.json` (52 transitive packages, ~3MB, pure JS + a wasm yoga layout engine — no headless browser, no native `.node` binary, nothing to add to the serverless bundle beyond JS; the PDF standard-14 Times faces are built in so there is no font file to ship either). Verified rendering server-side in plain Node with no DOM and under vitest's node environment. **New `src/lib/contract-pdf.js`:** `parseContractBlocks(markdown)` is a pure function splitting the frozen `body_rendered` into `{type:'heading',level,text}` / `{type:'list',items}` / `{type:'hr'}` / `{type:'paragraph',runs}`, with `**bold**` becoming `[{text,bold}]` runs via the also-exported `parseInlineRuns`. react-markdown (what `ContractBody.jsx` uses for the web view) could not be reused: it emits DOM elements react-pdf cannot render. GOVERNING RULE, stated in the file header: a legal document must never silently lose content, so every unrecognised construct (blockquotes, tables, links, raw HTML) renders as its literal source characters rather than being dropped — ugly beats missing. Two documented deviations from a strict `#`/`##`/`###` reading: `####`+ clamps to level 3 (ContractBody renders h4-h6 as real headings on the web, so a literal `#### x` in the PDF would diverge from the document the recipient signed, and clamping loses nothing), and an ordered-list line flushes the running paragraph so numbered clauses do not glue into one run-on block (still a `paragraph`, still carrying its literal `1. ` marker). `renderContractPdf({...})` maps those blocks onto react-pdf primitives via `React.createElement` (NOT JSX — a `.js` file under `src/lib` would hit vitest's esbuild `js` loader, which does not parse JSX) and returns a Buffer: A4, Times, 56pt margins, a fixed company-name/template-name header, a fixed `Page X of Y` footer, and a dual signature block matching the web layout (two 50% columns, "For \<companyName\>" / "Employee / Contractor", italic typed names, `Europe/Dublin` timestamps via the existing `formatFullDateTimeInTZ`, IP under the recipient). **Sign route:** after the status flip + detail fetch and before the emails, the whole PDF step (branding via `getLocationBranding` → render → `db.storage.from('contracts').upload('<id>/signed.pdf', buf, {contentType, upsert:true})` → awaited `signed_pdf_path` update) sits in one try/catch that pushes onto the route's existing `warnings` array. The sign itself can never fail on a PDF error — the DB row is the legal record and failing here would tell a recipient their signature did not go through when the row is already committed; the stale "deliberately deferred" comment was replaced with that reasoning. The buffer deliberately survives a STORAGE failure so the emails can still carry the attachment even when `/pdf` has nothing to serve yet. **Email:** `sendEmail` in `src/lib/postmark.js` gained a purely additive optional `attachments` passthrough (Postmark's own `[{Name, Content: base64, ContentType}]` shape; the `Attachments` key is only added when non-empty, so every existing caller's payload is byte-identical). `sendContractSignedEmails` gained an optional `pdfBuffer` — when present both the recipient and issuer emails attach `signed-contract.pdf`; over an 8MB raw ceiling (Postmark's cap is 10MB for the whole message and base64 inflates ~33%) the attachment is skipped and `results.warning` is set, which the sign route folds into its warnings, rather than losing the notification. **Download:** new `GET /api/contracts/[id]/pdf` mints a 60-second Supabase Storage signed URL and 302s to it — the `contracts` bucket (mig 106) stays private and no public URL is ever produced. Authorization mirrors `GET /api/contracts/[id]` exactly (recipient / master / org-owner, 404-not-403 so ids stay non-enumerable, plus the same draft-is-404-for-a-recipient-only-caller clause kept purely so the two gates cannot drift); `signed_pdf_path` null is also a 404. Registered in `src/lib/openapi.js` beside the other Contracts entries. Both `/admin/contracts/[id]` and `/account/contracts/[id]` render a "Download PDF" `print:hidden` link-button, styled like `ContractPrintButton`, only when `c.signed_pdf_path` is set (plain `<a>`, not `<Link>` — the 302 to Storage is a real navigation). **Tests:** 36 in `src/lib/contract-pdf.test.js` (headings incl. the `####` clamp and the `#tag` non-heading, list grouping/regrouping/interruption, hr forms incl. rejecting `--`, soft-wrap joining, CRLF, ordered-list splitting, bold runs incl. an unterminated `**` staying literal, empty/whitespace/non-string input, and a never-lose-a-line assertion over a realistic contract) plus two renderer smoke tests that assert a real non-empty `%PDF-` Buffer — the renderer runs fine under vitest, so nothing needed skipping; the PDF binary is deliberately NOT snapshotted (react-pdf embeds a creation timestamp). 10 route tests for the pdf GET (401, 404 foreign-staff + foreign-org-owner with `createSignedUrl` never called, 404 no-pdf-yet, 404 recipient-draft, 302 for recipient/org-owner/master, 500 on a failed mint with no Location header) and 4 email tests (attaches to both, absent when no buffer, oversized skip + warning, zero-length ignored). No migration (mig 106 already created the bucket and the `signed_pdf_path` column, both unused until now). Full local CI mirror green; `npm run build` left to CI. Not verified end to end against real Storage/Postmark. |
| 419 | CONTRACTS-BULK.1 — bulk issue: one template, many recipients | Issuing the same contract to N staff used to mean N full wizard runs. `ContractIssueWizard.jsx`'s recipient state became `profileIds` (array, was a single `profileId`) with a searchable checkbox multi-select at step 1 (text filter over `full_name`/`email`/`employment_type` + a bordered scrollable list, "Clear (N)"), replacing the single `<select>`; the B2 `?from=` re-issue prefill now does `setProfileIds([c.profile_id])`. New pure `eligibleTemplatesFor(recipients, templates)` in `src/lib/contracts.js` — a template qualifies only if it's `'both'` or matches EVERY selected recipient's `employment_type` (unknown/null type only ever matches `'both'`; empty selection = every template), used for the step-1 template list; a `useEffect` clears `templateId` if a recipient add/remove makes the current pick ineligible (guarded on both fetches — staff resolving every `profileId`, templates non-empty — so it can't race-clear a valid `?from=` prefill while `/api/staff`+`/api/contract-templates` are still in flight). Step 2 is visually unchanged; custom variables stay shared across all recipients, and the existing `unmappedKeys` (undeclared-placeholder) list keeps deriving from `recipients[0]` as before. New pure `unresolvedPlaceholdersUnion(bodyMarkdown, recipients, customVariables, opts)` runs `unresolvedPlaceholders()` per recipient and unions the results (order = first-encountered), remembering which recipient(s) each key belongs to — a key like `annual_salary` that auto-fills for one recipient but not another still surfaces, now named in the step-3 warning (e.g. "`{{annual_salary}}` (Sarah Doe)"); with one recipient this reduces to exactly the old single-recipient list (test-pinned). Step 3 renders one `renderTemplate()` preview per recipient (shared vars + that recipient's own profile vars + the existing location-var bracket stand-ins) behind a compact pager/tab strip (recipient-name pills + prev/next) when >1 recipient is selected; each tab's own remaining unresolved keys (not just the union) decide whether THAT tab shows raw-highlighted or formatted. B1 (CONTRACTS-EDIT.1) body-override editing is single-recipient only — the "Edit text" button is replaced by a plain hint ("Manual text edits are available when issuing to a single recipient.") once >1 recipient is selected; a single selection still gets the full untouched B1 editor. "Issue contract"/"Save as draft" become "Issue N contracts"/"Save N drafts" for N>1; clicking loops the existing `POST /api/contracts` sequentially per recipient (identical payload, minus `body_override`, which bulk never sends), replacing each pending row in a results list live as it settles. Exactly one recipient still calls the original `handleIssueSingle` path verbatim (redirect to the new contract + `sessionStorage` warning-stash, unchanged); >1 recipient never redirects — once a bulk run starts, the Save/Issue buttons are replaced by a live per-recipient status list (name + Waiting…/an "Open" link to `/admin/contracts/<id>`/the error message) and a summary line that doubles as the busy-progress indicator ("Issuing 3 of 5…"), with a "Retry failed" button (re-loops only the error rows, reusing the same issue-vs-draft choice) once the run completes with failures — this deliberately replaces rather than just disables the action buttons during a bulk run, so a completed batch can't be re-submitted whole and duplicate every recipient's contract. No new endpoint, no migration. Every non-submit control (checkbox rows, pager tabs, clear/retry buttons) is `type="button"`. Tests: `eligibleTemplatesFor` (empty/single-type/mixed-type/unknown-type/empty-template-list) and `unresolvedPlaceholdersUnion` (single-recipient parity, per-key union across recipients, recipient attribution, all-recipients-missing, opts.assumeKeys passthrough) in `contracts.test.js`. Full CI mirror green. |
| 418 | CONTRACTS-VARS.2 — per-variable defaults + location auto-fill vocabulary | Two additions to the contracts engine, both no-migration (`variables_schema` is JSONB). **Per-variable defaults:** `contractVariableDefSchema` gains optional `default` (string, omitted rather than persisted empty). `ContractTemplateForm.jsx`'s custom-variable row gained a "default" input (date type renders a date input, matching the wizard's own input rendering; grid re-spanned key/label/type/default/required/remove as 2/3/2/2/2/1 across 12). `ContractIssueWizard.jsx` seeds `vars` from a template's declared defaults whenever the SELECTED TEMPLATE actually changes (a `useEffect` keyed on the memoized `template` object, not on every render) — precedence is default < re-issue prefill < issuer typing: the B2 prefill's custom variables are stashed in a ref (`pendingPrefillVarsRef`) rather than set directly, since the two mount-time fetches (staff+templates vs. the prefill contract) resolve in either order and only the ref survives to whichever one resolves `template` last; issuer typing wins simply because nothing re-runs the effect until `templateId` changes again. Switching to a genuinely different template resets `vars` to just that template's defaults — old typed values don't leak across (this also means a recipient change, which blanks `templateId`, now clears `vars` too — a deliberate small behavior change, previously nothing reset it). **Location auto-fill vocabulary:** new pure `locationVariables({ location, branding })` in `src/lib/contracts.js` → `{ location_name, location_address, location_phone, location_email, company_name }`, each key present only when its source resolves non-empty (`location` from `public.locations`: name/address/phone/email; `branding` from the existing `getLocationBranding()`, which already inherits location → org → `'UN1T'`). `POST /api/contracts` extended the recipient's `profile_locations → location` embed with those four columns, resolves the SAME location row it already picks as `locationId`, fetches branding once, and folds `{ ...locVars, ...customVariables }` into `mergeVariables()`'s existing `customVariables` argument (signature unchanged; profile < location < custom is enforced by spread order, a same-keyed custom variable still wins) — so a draft saved via B2 gets identical treatment for free since it shares the render path. Server-side this needs no `assumeKeys`: location vars are substituted into `merged` BEFORE `renderTemplate()` runs, so by the time the CONTRACT-VARS.1 leftover check (`extractPlaceholders(bodyRendered)`) fires, a resolved location placeholder has already become real text — verified via a route test asserting `body_rendered` contains the substituted values and an unset location field still 400s as an unmapped key (never silently blank). `unresolvedPlaceholders()` gained an optional 4th arg `opts.assumeKeys` for the ONE place that does need it: the wizard, which has no way to resolve location values client-side. Checked `/api/staff` (`listStaffForUser` in `src/lib/staff.js`) — an admin caller (master/owner, the only roles that can reach the issue wizard) DOES get `profile_locations(*, locations(*))`, so the raw location fields are technically present nested three levels deep, but replicating the server's default-location-selection AND the location→org branding inheritance client-side would drift from `/api/contracts`' own logic and there's no flat single field to read anyway — left `/api/staff` untouched. Instead the wizard passes `LOCATION_VAR_KEYS` as `assumeKeys` (never shows as "still unmapped"/"unmapped variable" prompts) and its preview substitutes bracket stand-ins (`[location name]`, `[company name]`, etc.) via a `LOCATION_VAR_PREVIEW` map merged in below profile vars/above issuer-typed vars, plus a one-line hint under the preview ("Location details are filled in automatically when the contract is issued.", shown only when the template actually references a location var). B1 interaction: since a hand-edited `bodyOverride` is sent to the server verbatim with NO substitution, the textarea (seeded from `preview`) inherits the bracket placeholders as literal text — added a stronger inline warning when editing a template that uses location vars, telling the issuer to replace the brackets manually (the brackets don't match the `{{...}}` regex, so they never trip the override's own leftover check — intentional, they're plain text by the time an override exists, not template syntax). `PROFILE_VAR_HELP` gained a "Location" group documenting all five keys with samples. Tests: `locationVariables` (all-keys-present, omits-empty, no-source defensiveness), `mergeVariables` composed with `locationVariables()` at the route's own call-site pattern (custom > location > profile precedence), `unresolvedPlaceholders`'s `assumeKeys` behavior, and a new `POST /api/contracts` describe block covering location-var substitution into `body_rendered`/`variables_data`, a same-keyed custom variable overriding a location var, a mocked `getLocationBranding` override proving `company_name` flows from branding not the location row, and the unset-field 400. Full CI mirror green; no migration. |
| 417 | CONTRACTS-DRAFT.1 — draft status + re-issue prefill | The `contracts` status machine already allowed `draft` (`canTransition` in `src/lib/contracts.js`) but nothing used it, and revoking/declining left no fast path back to a fresh contract. `contractIssueSchema` gains `save_as_draft`; `POST /api/contracts` inserts with `status: 'draft'` and skips the email/push entirely when set, logging `contract.drafted` instead of `contract.issued` (`warning` stays undefined — nothing was attempted). Extracted the issue route's notify block (template-name lookup + `sendContractIssuedEmail` + push try/catch) into `notifyContractIssued({ db, contract, issuer })` (`src/lib/contracts-notify.js`) so it's shared with the new send path. New `POST /api/contracts/[id]/send` (draft → issued, `issued_at` refreshed to the real send time since the column is NOT NULL and the draft row already carries a creation-time value, `.eq('status','draft')` optimistic guard) fires `notifyContractIssued` — the recipient's actual first notification. New `POST /api/contracts/[id]/discard` (draft → revoked, `revoked_reason: 'Draft discarded'`, NO email — the recipient never knew it existed) logs `contract.discarded`; both mirror resend/revoke's guard shape (owner/master, org-scoped 404-not-403, registered in `openapi.js`). Tightened `/api/contracts/[id]/revoke`: `canTransition(draft, revoked)` is legal in the pure state machine but the route emails the recipient, so a draft hitting `/revoke` now 409s explicitly (was previously falling through — its `.in('status', [...])` update guard even included `'draft'`, a live bug) and must go through `/discard` instead. New `ContractDraftActions.jsx` ("Send now" / "Discard draft", mirrors `ContractResendButton`/`ContractRevokeButton`'s busy/error shape); `/admin/contracts/[id]/page.js` shows a purple "Draft — recipient not notified yet" banner + those actions for `status==='draft'`, owner/master only (Resend/Revoke already excluded drafts via the existing `canManage` gate, unchanged). Re-issue: `ContractRevokeButton` gains an optional `reissueAfter` prop — on success it routes to `/admin/contracts/issue?from=<id>` instead of refreshing; the detail page renders both a plain Revoke and a "Revoke & re-issue" instance for issued/viewed, and a plain "Re-issue" link for revoked/declined. The issue page (`src/app/admin/contracts/issue/page.js`) reads `?from=` via `searchParams` and passes it to the wizard as `fromContractId`; on mount the wizard `GET`s that contract (extended its `profile` embed with `annual_salary`/`hourly_rate`/`overtime_rate`/`contracted_hours_per_week` — needed to identify profile-derived keys, access model unchanged) and prefills recipient + template + custom variables via new pure `customVariablesFrom(variablesData, recipientProfile)` (`src/lib/contracts.js` — strips every `profileVariables()` key plus `today`, keeps everything else; a null profile only strips `today`). The rendered body is NEVER prefilled (a hand-edit only ever comes from the B1 editor on the current issue) — an info note ("Details restored from the previous contract. Review before issuing.") signals the prefill. Draft-exclusion audit for recipient-facing surfaces: `pending-contracts` already filtered to `issued`/`viewed` (no change); `/account/contracts` had NO status filter at all and would have leaked a draft into its "Archive" section — added `.neq('status','draft')`; `GET /api/contracts` (mobile's `listContracts()` — recipient's own list) had no status filter either — its owner-arm `.or()` now reads `and(profile_id.eq.X,status.neq.draft),organization_id.in.(...)` (the org arm is untouched — an owner still needs to see org drafts to send/discard them) and the plain-recipient branch gets `.neq('status','draft')` too; master's view stays fully unfiltered (existing omniscient-admin behaviour, out of scope here). Class fully closed on audit: `GET /api/contracts/[id]` now 404s a draft for a recipient-only caller too (added a second check after the existing recipient/master/owner gate — `contract.status === 'draft' && isRecipient && !user.isMaster && !isOrgOwner` — master/owner still resolve it, since they need the row to send/discard it and the re-issue wizard's `?from=<id>` prefill fetches this route as one of them) and `/account/contracts/[id]/page.js` (recipient-only, no admin branch) `notFound()`s unconditionally on `status === 'draft'` right after its existing `profile_id` guard. Both routes' issued→viewed first-view side effects were already gated on `status === 'issued'` (verified, unchanged) so neither could touch a draft even before this hardening — the fix closes the *read* path, not a status-mutation risk. Recipient-facing draft visibility is now 404 everywhere the class is reachable: list (`GET /api/contracts`, `/account/contracts`), pending-contracts, and both detail reads (API + page); mobile inherits all of it since it only calls the shared web routes. Tests: `send`/`discard` route tests (401/403/404-foreign/409-non-draft/200 + concurrency-guard + notify/email-skip assertions), `route.test.js` (top-level) extended for `save_as_draft` (status, no email/push, `contract.drafted` audit) and the new draft-exclusion GET scoping, `[id]/route.test.js` extended for the recipient-vs-owner/master draft-visibility split, `contracts.test.js` for `customVariablesFrom`. Full CI mirror green; no migration. |
| 416 | CONTRACTS-TPLVER.1 — contract template version history | PATCH `/api/contract-templates/[id]` has always bumped `version` on a `body_markdown` change (mig 106), but the OLD body was simply overwritten — history was write-only. New `contract_template_versions` table (mig 446 — write-only, NOT yet applied, orchestrator applies via Supabase MCP before merge) snapshots `template_id`/`version`/`body_markdown`/`variables_schema`/`changed_by` immediately before every overwrite; unique `(template_id, version)` makes a retry idempotent. PATCH's existing body-change preflight (`.select('version')`) now also pulls `body_markdown`/`variables_schema` and `.upsert()`s the archive row (`onConflict: 'template_id,version', ignoreDuplicates: true`) BEFORE the update runs; an archive failure 500s and the update never fires, so the version counter can't outrun its own audit trail. New `GET /api/contract-templates/[id]/versions` mirrors the parent template GET's app-layer org-scoping (SAAS-5 model — service-role route, master sees all, owner scoped to `getOwnerOrganizationIds`, foreign/missing ids collapse to 404). RLS on the new table: one `SELECT` policy (master OR org-owner via a subquery join to `contract_templates`, mirroring mig 106/167's owner predicate) — no insert/update/delete policies, service-role-only writes. `/admin/contracts/templates/[id]` gained a read-only "History" section below the form: expandable `<details>` rows (`v{version}`, Dublin-formatted timestamp, `changed_by` full name) showing the raw archived `body_markdown` in a `<pre>` plus the variable list; "No previous versions" when empty. Also closes a pre-existing gap on that same page: the template fetch itself had NO org-scoping at all (unlike the sibling `/admin/contracts/templates` list page and `/admin/contracts/[id]` detail page) — a foreign-org owner could view another org's template body by guessing its id. Now CONTRACTS-SCOPE.1-scoped (`user.activeOrganization?.id`, `notFound()` for non-master-with-no-org or a foreign org, matching `/admin/contracts/[id]`'s pattern exactly); the new History fetch inherits this for free since it only runs after the scoped template read succeeds. `contract-templates` routes aren't yet registered in `src/lib/openapi.js` at all (no existing entry to mirror for the new versions route, so none was added). |

| 411 | E2E-SMOKE.1 — Playwright browser smoke pack (scheduled, read-only, prod-safe) | External review (Codex 2026-07-25) P2: no browser journey coverage — a broken login flow or a client crash on /invoices could ship green. NOT in the six-check CI mirror or per-PR pipeline; runs from `.github/workflows/e2e-smoke.yml` daily 07:00 UTC + workflow_dispatch. **INERT until secrets exist**: without `E2E_SMOKE_EMAIL`/`E2E_SMOKE_PASSWORD` (repo Actions secrets → a dedicated smoke user with whatsapp/invoices/pipeline permissions) the workflow exits green with a loud SKIPPED warning, and a bare local `npm run test:e2e` self-skips (verified). Journeys themselves are UNVERIFIED against a live target until the smoke user exists. |

The three conversation list routes now accept `?q=` (2+ chars): a scoped contact-id lookup (name/first_name/phone/wa_phone/email — two-step because embedded-resource filters are unreliable, CLAUDE.md invariant) OR'd with the thread's own denormalised fields (wa_phone/wa_profile_name, ig_username/customer_name, counterpart_email/counterpart_name/subject, + last_message_preview; columns verified against live information_schema). Pure or-string building + `,()`-stripping/`%_`-escaping in `src/lib/inbox-search-server.js` (test-pinned). UnifiedInbox: 300ms-debounced server search when q≥2; while active, rows = server hits ∪ client matches (dedup by channel:id), queue chips bypassed (search is global), and server hits are NOT re-filtered client-side (they can match on contact phone/email which the client haystack lacks); <2 chars falls back to the instant client filter exactly as before. |

External review (Codex 2026-07-25) P1: `instrumentation.js onRequestError` only fires for errors that ESCAPE Next — a route that catches an upstream error and returns 500/502 itself never registered, so five real prod 5xx (Xero contacts ×3, invoice approval ×2) left zero error_events rows. The storm-guarded persist is extracted to `src/lib/error-events.js` (`recordErrorEvent`, shared per-instance 30/min cap across BOTH producers; instrumentation.js now delegates to it) plus `serverErrorResponse({module,error,request,status,publicMessage})` — a drop-in for bare `NextResponse.json({success:false,error},{status:5xx})` catch-sites that logs via log.js, persists a correlated `handled` row, and returns the IDENTICAL public body/status. Adopted at the observed failure sites: `locations/[id]/xero/contacts` (DB read 500), `locations/[id]/xero/sync-contacts` (Xero pull catch — incl. 401 token-death/429, the silent-killer class), and all four 5xx sites in `invoices-inbox/[id]/data-approve` (step 500, push 502, attachment 502, stamp 500). Other catch-sites adopt opportunistically as touched — no blanket rewrite of 667 routes. Helper behaviour pinned in `error-events.test.js` (10 tests incl. storm-guard window + never-throws); route delegation pinned in the xero contacts route test. |

| 407 | MIA-REVIEW.3 — Mia tools correctness, followup/handoff safety, eval coverage (20 review findings) | No migration. **Dublin class times (3.1):** `formatDublinClassTime` extracted to `src/lib/agent/dublin-format.js` (booking-tools re-exports it, so its public surface is unchanged) and `formatNextClass` now returns a Dublin wall-clock label — `get_my_next_class` was handing the model a raw UTC ISO string, i.e. the documented mis-hour bug (a 7am summer class relayed as "6am", live 2026-06-12) still live in account-tools; the test that pinned the UTC output is updated. **Event auth (3.4, deliberate auth change):** `cancel_event_registration` and `reschedule_event_wave` now hard-require `verifiedContactId` like `cancel_class_booking`, and `book_event` requires verification when the bound contact is an existing MEMBER (`glofox_member_id`); a brand-new person entering a FREE event with name+email stays verification-free (the designed consultation-style flow). Auth model documented in the file header. **verify_identity linked path (3.5):** keyed on `ctx.channel === 'whatsapp' && !!contactId` — the email-only relaxation is justified by the phone factor, and `instagram_conversations.contact_id` exists, so `!!contactId` would silently downgrade any future IG contact link to email-only. **Clean tool errors (3.13):** the queue-insert failures return a fixed model-directed message and `console.error` the raw PostgREST string (card-tools convention) instead of putting constraint/column detail in the model's context. **ilike escaping (3.15):** `escapeLikePattern` escapes `%`/`_`/`\\` in customer-supplied email before `.ilike` (never a bypass — `emailPathVerifies` re-checks equality — but `_` matched any character and could fetch the wrong single row) and the candidate pool went `.limit(1)` → `.limit(5)`. **Audit ordering (3.14):** auto-mode `book_class`/`cancel_class_booking` write the `agent_membership_requests` intent row (status `pending`, `details.stage='executing'`) BEFORE the Glofox call and finalise it after (`finalizeBookingRequest`, loud on failure, falls back to a fresh insert) — a crash between the Glofox call and the log used to leave a real booking with no audit row at all. **Check-in pause guard (3.2):** `runFirstClassCheckins` selects `agent_active` + `agent_paused_at` and skips paused/inactive threads, mirroring the followup candidate query — the sticky operator pause (mig 435) deliberately doesn't stamp `agent_handed_off_at`, so Mia could message into a thread an operator had just paused. **SLA re-arm (3.6):** `handoff_escalated_at` is cleared by `resolveRearmPatch`, the cooldown re-arm and `handoff()` — it was written and never cleared, so a conversation could escalate only ONCE in its lifetime and every later handoff breached silently. **SLA sweep vs disabled agent (3.7):** new pure `shouldSweepLocation(settings)` gates on "has a customer_agent config" + `slaMinutes > 0`, not on `enabled` — `enabled=false` is the documented panic response to an agent incident, so the operator action most likely to coincide with unattended handoffs used to silence their escalation (manual takeovers still never escalate: the operator's own message is the human reply). **Quiet hours for proactive sends (3.17):** new pure `proactiveWindowOpen(nowMs, settings)` intersects the hard 09:00-19:59 Dublin band with the operator's `quiet_hours`; both runners consult it and log a structured skip reason. **Check-in cap (3.19):** the daily cap counts actual SENDS (from the `activities` rows' via label, `countCheckinSends`) instead of `first_class_checkin_at` stamps, which are also written for consent/already-discussed skips and used to eat the cap; falls back to the old stamp count if that read fails. **Approval crash recovery (3.18):** new pure `src/lib/agent/request-recovery.js` — the claim stamps `details.execution = {stage:'executing', started_at, by}` before the side effect and closes it to `done` after; a row stuck at `approved`+`executing` for >5min is a crashed approval, is re-claimable via a retry PATCH (claim predicate moves to the stale `started_at`, so the double-execution guard is unchanged) and now surfaces in a "Needs a retry" section on `/settings/customer-agent/requests`. **Decision log (3.16, partial):** `agent_decisions` rows now carry the ACTING (verified/person-primary) contact id rather than just the thread's contact. The rest of the finding (per-turn tool trace + inputs + model `stop_reason`) is NOT shipped: `agent_decisions` (mig 436) has no JSONB payload column and this batch ships no migration — it needs one. **Evals:** `buildScenarioRequest` forwards `multipleAccounts` + `knownContact` (the AGENT-AUTH.3 and known-contact prompt blocks were structurally unreachable from the harness) and builds the system as production's cached BLOCKS via `buildCachedSystem`; the request carries `output_config.effort` (default `DEFAULT_AGENT_EFFORT`, `AGENT_EFFORT=low npm run eval:agent` to validate an effort change before flipping it in production) (3.3/3.12). New scenarios: `dupe-phone-ask-which-email` (never read out an on-file name/address), `known-contact-never-re-asks`, `billing-hands-off`, `injury-hands-off`, `asks-for-human` (3.10), `cancel-confirm-before-cancel`, `cancel-refusal-relayed-honestly`, `reschedule-cancels-then-books` (3.11). `assertions.js` gained an options class (`optionsRequired`/`optionsMatch`/`optionsNotMatch`, count 2-10 + 20-char caps) so the `[[OPTIONS]]` payload the runner always captured is finally graded — booking-confirm requires buttons, full-class asserts the FULL class never lands on one (3.20). **Tests:** `enabled=true`+`test_mode=true` is pinned as LIVE-for-everyone with the invariant cited (3.8), and a new integration test drives the verify-fail auto-handoff through the real orchestration (threshold, counter increment/reset, 0-disables) which no test or eval touched before (3.9); plus new suites for the check-in pause guards, the audit ordering, and the recovery helpers. |
| 406 | MIA-REVIEW.2 — agent concurrency + Anthropic API robustness (15 review findings) | No migration. **Double-turn race (2.1/2.2):** `claimAgentTurn` now returns the stamp it wrote; the turn **heartbeats** `agent_processing_at` before every model attempt (`heartbeatAgentTurn`) and `releaseAgentTurn` is **compare-and-clear** (`.eq('agent_processing_at', stamp)`) — a finished-but-reclaimed turn can no longer wipe the reclaimer's claim, and `STALE_CLAIM_MS` (90s) goes back to being pure crash recovery. Every agent model call carries `AbortSignal.timeout(60s)`; a lost heartbeat stands the turn down as `claim_lost`. **Retries (2.4):** new `callAgentModel` wrapper — 429/5xx/network/abort retried up to 3 attempts with jittered backoff (`retry-after` honoured, capped 5s); non-429 4xx still escalates immediately. **stop_reason (2.3/2.9):** `max_tokens` retries once at 600→1000 then soft-hands-off as `model_truncated` (never sends a cut-off reply that could split a `[[OPTIONS]]`/`[[HANDOFF]]` sentinel); `refusal` hands off as `model_refusal`; `pause_turn` noted for the day a server-side tool is added. Distinct manager-push copy per cause. **Tool throws (2.8):** each executor call is wrapped and returned to the model as an `is_error` `tool_result`; the same tool throwing twice abandons the turn as `tool_error` instead of the misleading "model API failure" page. **Inbound persistence (2.5):** WA route + `instagram.js` now read `{ error }` on the inbound insert, log loudly and **skip the agent turn** (still 200 to Meta) so Mia never answers history missing the triggering message. **Reactions (2.6):** `shouldAgentReply` returns `ignorable_type` for `reaction` — a 👍 no longer earns the customer a holding message and staff a false "sent a photo" page (image/audio/video/document/location/contacts unchanged). **IG create race (2.7):** the loser of the first-message conversation-create race re-reads the winner's row and proceeds instead of dropping the message permanently (its `msg:<mid>` dedup row already suppresses Meta's retry). **WA dedup (2.14):** cheap pre-insert `wa_message_id` + `direction=inbound` lookup as a second layer behind the fail-open `recordWebhookEvent` (`idx_wa_messages_wa_id` is non-unique; no migration). **Debounce (2.12):** `softHandoff` now wins `agent_last_reply_at` with a conditional update before sending, so parallel webhook POSTs can't double-page. **Dead air (2.15):** `location_daily_cap` and `send_failed` page managers, debounced off `agent_decisions` (per day / per hour) so a silent cap or a dead token is visible. **Metering (2.10):** `recordUsage` is awaited inside its own try/catch (Vercel doesn't guarantee unawaited promises complete). **Cache (2.11):** a moving `cache_control` breakpoint on the newest `tool_result` so intra-turn iterations read the accumulated prefix from cache. **Tripwire (2.13):** shared `resolveAgentGate()` used by `shouldAgentReply` + `shouldSendWelcome`, plus `warnLiveDespiteTestMode()` (once per process per location) and a `live_despite_test_mode` flag on the settings GET + a UI warning — semantics unchanged, `enabled`+`test_mode` is still live for everyone. 22 new tests (claim protocol, retry classification, stop_reason branches, tool-error recovery, atomic debounce, dead-air pages, reaction no-op, IG race, WA dedup/insert-skip). Full CI mirror (8716 tests) + `next build` green. |
| 405 | MIA-REVIEW.1 — Mia invariant compliance: capacity secrecy, prompt rules, customer copy | Code-review batch on the customer agent (no migration; the two new settings fields live in the existing `locations.settings.customer_agent` JSONB). **Capacity secrecy (owner invariant 2):** `shapeClassListForAgent` / `shapeEventsForAgent` no longer return `spots_left` at all — the model gets `full` plus a coarse `limited` (≤3 places) and can therefore never relay a count (same posture as the public signup widget's `is_full`, #94); tool descriptions and the events prompt line ("Relay dates, waves and spaces naturally") updated, and a hard prompt rule now bans stating spots/spaces/places. | 399 | Mia — contextual disambiguation when a number is linked to more than one account | When a WhatsApp number maps to more than one PERSON, `resolveAutoVerify` can't pick an account, so it fell back to the blind "confirm the email on your membership and your surname" quiz — with no context, and re-triggered by **any** single ungrouped duplicate on the number (dedupe isn't scalable). Now Mia acknowledges the situation and asks WHICH account by email: new pure helper `core.distinctPersonCount(matches, groupOf)` (a person = person-group id if grouped, else the contact id, so duplicate rows already linked as one Person count once) computed in `auto-reply.js` from the existing phone-match set + group resolver; `≥2` sets a new `multipleAccounts` prompt flag. `prompt.js` gains an `else if (multipleAccounts)` volatile block (after the pre-verified block, which still wins) instructing Mia to say there's more than one account on the number and ask them to confirm the **email** on the one they mean (email ONLY, no surname — the phone is already a factor), then call `verify_identity` with it. Crucially it must **never read out, list, or hint at any name/email on file** (no PII leak in the genuine two-people-on-one-number case); the customer supplies the email, and `verify_identity` (since #1011) matches it across all accounts on the number and resolves to that person's primary. Scope: WhatsApp only where the number is ambiguous — Instagram / unknown / single-account numbers keep the existing flow. Tests: `distinctPersonCount` (linked-dupes collapse to 1, ungrouped stray → 2, two groups → 2, malformed/empty) + prompt-branch (asks email-with-context, no on-file reveal, yields to pre-verified, off by default). Full CI mirror + `next build` green. Root-caused from Richard's number after a 5th ungrouped "test" contact re-tripped auto-verify post-#1011. |
| 398 | Mia — auto-hand-off after repeated identity-verification failure | After **N (default 2)** consecutive failed `verify_identity` attempts in a conversation, Mia stops re-asking and hands off to a human instead of looping the email+surname quiz (Richard hit a 3× loop). **Mig 433** (applied) adds `agent_verify_attempts smallint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` to `whatsapp_conversations` + `instagram_conversations`. Counting lives in pure `core.js` helpers (`resolveVerifyFailHandoff` — settings `handoff_after_verify_failures`, 0 disables; `shouldHandoffAfterVerifyFail`; `nextVerifyAttempts`); `auto-reply.js` seeds the counter from the conversation, updates it per `verify_identity` result in the tool loop (reset on success, +1 on failure), and after the loop — if the threshold is met — calls the existing `handoff()` (reason `verify_failed`; standard holding message + manager page + SLA re-alert), discarding the model's retry text. Counter resets on success, on the forced handoff, and on agent re-arm. Only the email-quiz path is affected (phone-auto-verified senders never call `verify_identity`); WhatsApp + Instagram via the shared loop. No settings-UI field in v1 (tunable via the settings blob). Follows #1011 (the unwinnable-quiz fix) — now the residual "genuinely can't ID this person" cases reach a human instead of looping. Full CI mirror + `next build` green. |
| 397 | Mia — fix unwinnable identity quiz when a WhatsApp number has duplicate contacts | A member texting from a number with several contact rows could be permanently unable to verify: `verify_identity`'s linked path (`src/lib/agent/account-tools.js`) matched the provided email against **only the thread's bound `contact_id`**. When the conversation was pinned to a duplicate whose email differed from the member's (e.g. a `test@` dupe with no Glofox membership sitting on the same number as the real `member` row), the customer's correct membership email could never match → "I can't match those details to an account", no email they could truthfully give would work. Now the linked path builds a candidate **pool of every contact sharing the sender's WhatsApp/phone number at the location** (new IO helper `contactsForSender`, mirroring `core.resolveAutoVerify`'s phone match) and verifies if the email matches **any** of them (new pure helper `pickVerifiedContact`; single-contact pools behave exactly as before, so it's a strict, safe broadening — the number is still the second factor, the customer can only type their own email). Stamps the matched contact; auto-reply still resolves it to the person-group primary for the acting account. Unlinked (Instagram / unknown number) path unchanged — still email + surname. Root-caused from Richard's own thread (number `353…7675` had 4 Richard contacts, 3 person-linked + 1 stray `test@` dupe the thread was bound to; the stray was also person-linked as an immediate data fix). Tests: 8 new (pool matching across a sibling dupe, case-insensitivity, no-match, single-contact parity, unlinked email+surname, nameHint; executor stamps the MEMBER not the bound dupe, and still refuses a foreign email). Full CI mirror green. |

| 397 | Repset: plan assignment — pin a tier/add-on to a tenant location (the billing on-switch) | **The missing keystone that makes the already-built billing/wallet/enforcement machinery operable.** Billing is PER-LOCATION: "allocate a plan to a tenant" = pin a plan VERSION to one of the tenant's LOCATIONS via a `location_plans` row (mig 413). Nothing wrote `location_plans` before — every location was unpinned, so `getLocationPlan()` returned null and wallets/enforcement/monthly-reset/meters/billing-page/MRR all sat dormant. Pinning an ACTIVE TIER is the on-switch: `getLocationPlan()` starts returning non-null → the whole billing machine engages **for that location only**. **Write route `POST /api/admin/tenants/[orgId]/plans`** (master-only, mirroring the sibling `/api/admin/tenants` routes' `getCurrentUser`→`profileRole==='master'`→401/403 guard). Body `{ location_id, plan_id|plan_slug, plan_version_id?, kind }`; `plan_version_id` defaults to the plan's CURRENT active version (`getActivePlanVersion`, explicit grandfathering by passing a specific version). Validates: location belongs to `orgId` (**cross-org / unknown → 404 not 403**, IDOR rule); plan exists, is `active`, and its `kind` matches the body; an explicit version belongs to the plan. **THE ONE-ACTIVE-TIER INVARIANT (load-bearing, enforced SERVER-SIDE):** assigning a tier calls `deactivateActiveTierPins()` — a single guarded `UPDATE location_plans SET active=false WHERE location_id=… AND active=true AND plan_version_id IN (<tier version ids>)` — **BEFORE** activating the new tier (deactivate-first ordering), so the only reachable states are one active tier (success) or zero (interrupted → dormant, which `getLocationPlan` treats as unpinned); two active tiers is never reachable through this route. Add-ons are additive (multiple allowed, tier untouched) and idempotent (re-assigning the same add-on keeps exactly one active row). **Unassign `DELETE /api/admin/tenants/[orgId]/plans`** `{ location_id, plan_version_id }` → deactivates that pin; unpinning the tier returns the location to DORMANT (`getLocationPlan` null, billing off), unpinning an add-on just drops it; unknown pin / cross-org → 404. Both registered in `openapi.js`. **UI on the tenant drill-in** (`/admin/tenants/[orgId]`, `TenantDetailView.jsx`): each location shows its pin state ("No plan — dormant" vs active tier + version + price + "Billing active"), an **Assign/Change plan** modal (pick a tier from the active catalogue, version defaulted to current with price shown, consequence copy "activates billing, wallet and usage enforcement for this location"), **add-on toggles** (e.g. custom_email_domain), and a **Set dormant** action ("returns it to dormant — no billing"); every change `router.refresh()`es so pin/MRR/health re-grade immediately. `getTenantDetail` now also returns the assignable `catalogue` (active plans + their current version, via the new pure `buildPlanCatalogue`) and each location's `plan.planVersionId` + active add-on ids. **No migration** — `location_plans` (mig 413) already exists; this only adds the first WRITE path (wallet/enforcement/cron code that reads `location_plans` is untouched). Tests: one-active-tier atomicity (2nd/3rd tier → assert EXACTLY ONE active tier after), add-on additivity + idempotency, validation (location-in-org, active plan, kind match, version-on-plan, cross-org 404), master-only 401/403, default-version resolution, unassign→no active tier, other-locations-unaffected; plus `buildPlanCatalogue` unit tests. Full CI mirror + `next build`. |
| 395 | REPSET-PLATFORM.3 — tenant support access (read-only + act-on-behalf, audited) | **Phase 3 of the Repset platform-architecture work: lets a MASTER "view into" any tenant from the Platform Console to help them, in two modes, audited.** Built ON TOP of the existing master-impersonation mechanism (`src/lib/impersonation.js`) — a support session opens against a tenant ORGANIZATION and REUSES `startImpersonation()` to swap identity/scope to an owner of that org (org_admin > owner-at-a-location; scope-only fallback when the org has no owner profile), so the master sees the tenant's `/portfolio` account home + studios exactly as they would; the active-location cookie is scoped to a location in the org so the portfolio resolves to that tenant deterministically. State is a signed `un1t_support` cookie (`{sid,org,mode,master,imp,iat,exp}`) minted alongside the impersonation cookie, NOT a parallel identity system. **READ-ONLY ENFORCEMENT — the security crux, server-side + FAIL-CLOSED at the CENTRAL chokepoint `src/proxy.js`.** New block runs BEFORE the public-path/Bearer gates (so nothing mutating slips past) and covers BOTH `/api/*` mutations AND Next.js server-action POSTs to page routes (both arrive as POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE): `decideSupportWriteBlock(support, method, path)` (pure, exported + unit-tested in `src/lib/support-session-edge.js`) returns block=true unless — no active support session, OR a safe method (GET/HEAD/OPTIONS), OR the mode is EXACTLY `act_on_behalf`, OR the path is a support **control route** (`/api/support-session/exit`, `/api/support-session/switch`, `/api/impersonate/stop` — allowlisted so a master can always leave/upgrade). **Fail-closed:** a write is allowed ONLY for an EXPLICIT `act_on_behalf`; a present-but-unverifiable (tampered/corrupt) cookie resolves to read_only via `readSupportModeEdge` and blocks; an unknown/garbled mode blocks; ambiguity blocks → 403 `read_only_support_mode` with a UI-readable message. Cookie is HMAC-SHA-256 signed (Web Crypto, Edge-safe, secret = `SUPPORT_SESSION_SECRET` ‖ `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`) so it is tamper-evident. **Act-on-behalf** = writes allowed but scoped to the tenant via the reused impersonation (all existing IDOR/location guards keep the master inside that org). **Audit** = new **mig `431_support_sessions.sql`** (FILE ONLY — NOT applied; orchestrator applies via MCP): one open row per master (master id, target org id, impersonated user id, mode, reason, ip/ua, started/ended, auto_closed); RLS = master-only SELECT + RESTRICTIVE deny-writes (mig 413/420 shape), service-role writes. The hourly `close-stale-impersonations` cron now ALSO closes stale support sessions (shared 2h max-age, truthful upper-bound ended_at + auto_closed). `GET /api/support-session` (master-only) + a "Recent support sessions" panel on `/admin/tenants` surface the trail. **Banner** — `src/components/SupportBanner.jsx`: persistent "Support mode: READ-ONLY · <tenant>" (indigo) or "Acting on behalf of · <tenant>" (amber, writes LIVE), one-click mode switch + Exit (ends the session, returns to the Platform Console). Rendered in `AppShell` in place of the plain impersonation banner while a support session is active. **Master-only** throughout: start/switch/exit gate on the REAL underlying master (`impersonatingFrom.masterId ‖ profileRole==='master'`) → 403 otherwise; no new `WEB_PERMISSIONS` key (role-gated like the other master surfaces). Start action = per-row "View into ↗" mode picker in `TenantsConsole`. Existing impersonation guards/scoping + the studio/account/platform shells are UNCHANGED (banner added only). Tests: proxy read-only-blocks-writes decision (mutating→403, GET allowed, act_on_behalf allowed, control routes allowed, fail-closed on tamper) + sign/verify round-trip + stale-close planner + cross-tenant target selection. Full CI mirror + `next build`. Migration NOT applied — HELD FOR REVIEW. |
| 394 | REPSET-PLATFORM.1 — Platform Console shell + master lands on the cockpit | **Phase 2 of the Repset platform-architecture work: adds the PLATFORM tier** (Platform → Account → Studio), a master-only cross-tenant cockpit that gives `/admin/tenants` a real shell/home and gathers the four clearly-platform pages under it, separated from UN1T's own operational `/admin`. Mirrors the account-tier shell EXACTLY. **`src/lib/platform-nav.js`** — tested nav contract: `isPlatformTierPath(pathname)` claims ONLY the four console route roots (`/admin/tenants`, `/admin/plans`, `/admin/tenant-domains`, `/admin/health` — exact or nested `/...`, so the tenant drill-in and provision wizard stay shelled) + `resolvePlatformNav(user)` (a **Console** section = the four pages, Tenants as the home; an **Actions** section = **Provision tenant** → `/admin/tenants/new` LIVE and inside the shell, **Audit log** → `/admin/audit-log` LIVE as a cross-link OUT of the console — it keeps its current studio shell per the Phase-2 rule that nothing else in /admin moves; both included because their routes exist in-repo, an absent route would be OMITTED not shown as a dead row; plus an explicit **"Exit to app →"** affordance to `/dashboard`). **`src/components/platform/PlatformShell.jsx`** renders it, mirroring `AccountShell.jsx`/`Sidebar.jsx` (UN1T monochrome, same `<aside>`/tokens/user-logout block); header reads "Repset · Platform". Wired via a single additive branch in **`AppShell.jsx`**: the three shells are now **mutually exclusive** — `isPlatformTier = !!user.isMaster && isPlatformTierPath(pathname)`, `isAccountTier = !isPlatformTier && isAccountTierPath(pathname)`, else studio (default). On a console path FOR A MASTER it renders `PlatformShell` instead of `Sidebar`+`CommandPalette` and drops the studio-only `AssistantBubble`; on every studio path both flags are false and the chrome renders **byte-for-byte** as before. **Master gating**: the console branch engages ONLY for `user.isMaster` — a non-master who somehow hits a console path falls through to the normal shell, and the four pages' OWN master page-guards (unchanged — not weakened) then redirect them to `/`. **Master landing** (`resolveLandingTarget` in `dashboard-redirect.js`, applied ONLY at root `/`): a master now lands on **`/admin/tenants`** (the console home) instead of `/portfolio` — unconditional (the console is cross-tenant, no active org required). Fail-safe (try/catch → null) + loop-safe (`/admin/tenants` isn't `/` so it never re-runs the resolver, and its master page-guard passes a master through without redirecting). Owners/multi-studio (non-master) still land on `/portfolio` per Phase 1; single-studio unchanged. **ONLY the four pages were gathered** — checklists, tv-displays, studio-devices, bridges, contracts, policies, matrix, audit-log, achievements, imports keep their current routes AND their current shell. No migration; master-only; the studio + account shells are untouched. Tests: `isPlatformTierPath` (four roots, nested, prefix false-positives, other /admin pages excluded) + `resolvePlatformNav` (console order, live cross-links, insideShell flags, exit route) + the master-landing decision (master→/admin/tenants incl. no-org + profileRole detection). Full CI mirror + `next build`. |

| 392 | REPSET-ACCOUNT.1 — Account Home (org portfolio) + multi-studio login landing | **Phase 1 of the Repset platform-architecture work: adds the ACCOUNT tier** (Platform → **Account = an `organization`** → Studio = a `location`), an org-level home that rolls up across a company's studios then drills into one. Deliberately NOT applied on `/dashboard` — that stays the studio drill-in target, so "Open studio → /dashboard" can't bounce back to `/portfolio` (**loop-safe**: `/portfolio` only ever redirects to `/dashboard`, which never redirects here). A **WhatsApp** panel — the shared Connect card + per-number **Disconnect** calling the SAME `DELETE /api/locations/[id]/whatsapp/numbers/[numberId]` the tab's `remove()` calls, with the tab's EXACT confirm() copy + semantics (targets only that number's id, never reactivates, never touches a different number); numbers held in local state so a disconnect updates the panel while `onChanged` re-grades the hub card behind it. Heavy config stays one click away on the footer deep-link ("Xero data & accounts" / "Manage numbers"). **(3) Hub wiring** (`IntegrationsHub.jsx`): the Xero Connect/Manage/Reconnect and each WhatsApp row's Connect/Manage now `setManaging({cardKey, locationId, initial})` instead of navigating; re-grade reuses the existing `onChanged`→`GET /api/integrations/hub` refetch. WhatsApp is the LIVE customer channel — no send/routing/activation code touched; connect + per-number disconnect only. | 390 | INTEG hub inline #4 (Phase 2) — in-hub Manage for Glofox/Twilio/UniFi/AC/BCA (write-only merge, Glofox null-collapse guarded) | Extends the Phase-1 Manage drawer (`IntegrationsHubDrawer.jsx`) with first-class inline credential forms for the five providers stored on the `locations` row, saving through ONE new service-role route **`PUT`/`DELETE /api/locations/[id]/integrations/[provider]`** (provider ∈ glofox\|twilio\|unifi\|ac\|bca) with a per-provider descriptor (fields, secret fields, role tier, storage layout). **New pure helper `src/lib/integration-secret-merge.js`** (`mergeSecretSlice`/`sliceHasValue`/`isFreshSecret`/`maskSecret`): given {stored slice, incoming patch, secretFields} it returns the merged slice where a blank OR masked-echo secret KEEPS the stored value, a fresh value overwrites, non-secret fields set normally. **Glofox null-collapse guard** — the old tab computed `glofox: (anyFieldTruthy ? {…} : null)`; with inline fields starting blank/masked a naive port would compute "all empty → null" and WIPE Stillorgan's LIVE Glofox connection (the only Glofox-connected location). The merge carries stored secrets forward so a no-op save yields a NON-EMPTY slice; the "empty→null" collapse (via `sliceHasValue`, judged on the STORED slice not the blank inputs) can only fire when nothing was stored; clearing happens ONLY through the explicit DELETE disconnect. **Route** (mirrors `…/connections/refresh`): `getCurrentUser`→401 → `assertLocationAccess`→403 → per-provider role gate (glofox/twilio = `ADMIN_ROLES`; unifi/ac/bca = **master-only**, matching the tabs' `canEdit={isMaster}` + the mig-034 UniFi trigger which service-role writes bypass) → `validateBody` (Zod) → read the stored slice → merge → read-merge-write the `locations` field (sibling `settings` slices untouched) → `syncConnectionFromLegacy()` IN-HANDLER (collapses the old fire-and-forget refresh race) → return a MASKED echo (`has_*` + non-secret values, never a token). DELETE clears the slice + syncs → registry row DEACTIVATED (`is_active=false`, not hard-deleted, no provider-side revoke). AC writes 4 columns + re-syncs BOTH `sensibo` and `thinq` platforms; BCA (`bca_config` — audited: NO secrets) merges the email/template fields, preserves + validates document slots via `validateBcaConfig`, feature-gated on `bca_submit`. **Assembler** (`integrations-hub.js`) gains per-provider presence booleans + non-secret prefill (glofox `{branchId,namespace,hasApiKey,hasApiToken,hasWebhookSecret}`, unifi `{host,hasToken,staffPolicyId,managerPolicyId,allowSelfSigned}`, climate `{sensibo:{hasKey},thinq:{hasPat,clientId,countryCode}}`, sms `{senderId}`, bca `{sendFrom,sendTo,cc,…}`) mapped to booleans — no secret value is selected into the payload (test asserts no raw secret appears in the assembled JSON). **Drawer**: masked/empty secret inputs ("leave blank to keep"), non-secret fields prefilled, Save→PUT + explicit Disconnect→DELETE, `onChanged`→refetch `GET /api/integrations/hub`; UniFi/AC/BCA render READ-ONLY for a non-master viewer. Registered in `openapi.js`. Tests: `integration-secret-merge.test.js` (18 — the four Glofox cases: blank save preserves the live connection, fresh key isolates, masked-echo=keep, empty-slice collapse) + the route's `route.test.js` (10 — end-to-end null-collapse keeps the registry active, disconnect deactivates, role gates, sibling non-clobber, secret non-leak) + assembler presence test. No migration (has_* derived in the assembler). |
