| 348 | QSTASH.10 — QStash queued push (parallelism 1) for receipt hunts | Eighth job onto the QSTASH.1 push pattern, third on a **bounded QStash queue** and the first at **parallelism 1**: the RCOV.P1 hunt engine has always drained its queue strictly sequentially (a hunt opens IMAP sessions against the location's operator mailboxes + burns a Claude Vision call per candidate), so publishes go onto the `receipt-hunts` FIFO queue with parallelism 1 — sequentiality preserved exactly, latency drops from */5-min polling to continuous drain. Enqueue site: `seedHunts` (`src/lib/recon/statuses.js`, the Friday receipt-coverage-weekly seed step, the ONLY writer of `hunt_queued_at`) allSettled-publishes `{ id }` per seeded row (dedup `receipt-hunt-<id>`, dash-only) — **capped at new exported `HUNT_PUBLISH_CAP` 200 per seed call**, because the Friday seed is bulk and QStash's free tier caps 1000 req/day: a normal week seeds a handful of lines (per hunt.js's own volume notes) but a pathological backlog could seed hundreds; ~2 Xero-connected locations → ≤400 publishes worst-case, overflow rows swept by the cron exactly as pre-QStash. Claim: new `src/lib/recon/hunt-queue.js` — `claimAndHuntLine` does a by-id CAS mirroring the `claim_recon_hunt_batch` RPC predicate (mig 370: status in uncovered/not_found + `hunt_queued_at` set + `hunt_claimed_at` NULL-or-stale >10 min) then runs the pre-existing shared per-row unit `huntLine` (unlike QSTASH.6 nothing needed extracting — the cron already called it); the RPC's SKIP-LOCKED batch claim + stale-claim crash recovery stay CRON-ONLY. Worker `/api/webhooks/qstash/receipt-hunts` (`maxDuration` 300, raw-body-first verify): **200 for EVERY hunt outcome including `error`** — `huntLine` never throws and its `errorFinish` already recorded a terminal `recon_hunts` audit row + de-queued the line, exactly the cron's stance (tallies `failed`, never retries; next Friday re-seeds); 500 only for row-fetch infrastructure errors. **Deliberate safety line: the weekly finalizer stays CRON-ONLY** — the worker NEVER calls `maybeFinalizeWeekly` (it emails the coverage report and stamps the receipt-coverage-weekly heartbeat, and its `reportAlreadySent` guard is check-then-act, not race-proof); worst case the report waits ≤5 min for the next process-receipt-hunts tick. Worker stamps no heartbeat; vercel.json + heartbeats untouched; no migration. openapi.js registered; INTEGRATIONS.md table row added. Tests: hunt-queue claim-CAS contract, worker response semantics, seedHunts publish hook (cap, zero-seed, publish-failure isolation). |
| 347 | QSTASH.9 — QStash push delivery for external export jobs (Strava) | Seventh job onto the QSTASH.1 push pattern and the second on a **bounded QStash queue**: publishes go onto the `strava-exports` FIFO queue with **parallelism 2**, not plain publish — each export burns 2–4 Strava API calls (token refresh + TCX upload + poll) against the app-wide 100-req/15-min budget, and the enqueue site fans out per member (live-class `endSession` is looped by `endAllAtLocation` for a whole class and by the auto-end-stale-hr-sessions sweep), so an "end all" on a 30-person class must never become 30 concurrent uploads. Enqueue site: `enqueueExportsForSession` in `src/lib/external-export.js` is the ONLY writer of new `external_export_jobs` rows (repo-sweep verified across src/, shared/, mobile/ — every other `.from('external_export_jobs')` is the worker's own claim/status bookkeeping; champ-app and champ-bridge share the DB but never touch the table) — its ignoreDuplicates upsert now chains `.select('id')` (ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING returns ONLY freshly-created rows) and allSettled-publishes `{ id }` per inserted job (dedup `strava-export-<id>`, dash-only, no delay); duplicates and the `markFailed` requeue path deliberately publish nothing — a re-queued row carries a future `next_attempt_at` and its retry belongs to the cron's backoff. Shared per-job unit: NO new lib file — `external-export.js` already is the queue's lib, so new exported `processExportJob(db, job)` wraps the previously-private `processOneJob` plus the cron loop's exact throw-path bookkeeping (markFailed: re-queue with the 1m→6h backoff under MAX_ATTEMPTS 5, terminal 'failed' + integration `last_error` bubble at the cap); `runExportWorker` now loops over it — behaviour byte-identical, and the claim semantics are PRESERVED, not upgraded: processOneJob claims by blindly flipping 'processing'+attempts+1 (no CAS — the documented accepted race; Strava de-dupes uploads by external_id). New worker `/api/webhooks/qstash/strava-exports` (maxDuration 60 — same as the cron; raw-body-first verify, 503/401 split): eligibility fetch is 'queued' AND `next_attempt_at` due, NOT 'processing' (a QStash timeout-redelivery mid-upload must 200-skip while the original invocation finishes; crashed-'processing' recovery stays CRON-ONLY via the cron's batch select which includes 'processing'). **Deliberate failure contract (the invoice-analysis stance, for a different reason): job failures return 200, not 500** — the bookkeeping already re-queued the row on the queue's own schedule (or went terminal), and because the claim is not a CAS a late QStash retry would race the cron into a duplicate upload while burning Strava rate budget; 500 is reserved for infrastructure errors (row fetch) where nothing ran and a retry helps. Strava API behaviour untouched (personal-scope ToS constraint) — only delivery timing changes: ~2-min cron latency → seconds. Cron header → sweeper; vercel.json + heartbeat untouched. Kill switch: unset `QSTASH_TOKEN`. openapi.js registered (`{ id: uuidLike }`); INTEGRATIONS.md intro + table row. 27 new tests (publish hook: exact queue shape, dash-only dedup, inserted-rows-only, no-publish on upsert error, rejection isolation, per-provider fan-out; processExportJob: claim flip + attempts bump, terminal skip, requeue-with-backoff ≥60s, at-cap terminal + integration bubble, refresh-failure path; worker: constants, maxDuration 60, verify mapping, 200-on-failure contract), TDD'd red-first. No migration. |
| 346 | QSTASH.8 — QStash campaign-kick delivery for host campaign sends | Sixth job onto the QSTASH.1 push pattern and the first BULK-SEND job — the message granularity changes from one-per-queue-row to **one campaign-level kick + worker chunk-chaining** (a per-recipient publish for a thousands-row campaign would burn the QStash free-tier 1000-requests/day budget and lose the cron's pacing). Enqueue site: `POST /api/host/emails/[id]/send` — the ONLY site that flips a `host_campaigns` row into 'sending' (repo-sweep verified: the cron only reads/finalises; the email-domain verify routes flip `sender_domain_verified`, never campaign status — a re-verified halted campaign resumes via the cron, deliberately no publish) — fire-and-forget publishes `{ campaignId }` (dedup `host-campaign-<id>-kick`, dash-only) AFTER the full fan-out enqueue; ordering is load-bearing (a kick delivered before the pending rows land would look "drained" and mis-finalise the campaign — the same latent race the cron's 2-min cadence made unhittable), so the partial-enqueue 500 path publishes nothing. New shared lib `src/lib/host-campaign-queue.js` (`processHostCampaignChunk(db, campaignId)`) extracts the cron's per-campaign tick: re-verify sender EVERY chunk (the UN1T kill switch stops in-flight campaigns → 'halted': no error, no finalise, campaign stays 'sending'), CAS-claim ≤50 pending rows (pending→claimed conditioned on still-pending — overlapping consumers win disjoint rows, LOAD-BEARING), send-time consent + per-host-suppression re-check, Postmark broadcast send from the host's verified sender, per-row sent/failed stamps, re-derived sent_count, finalise only when nothing pending AND nothing claimed. Statuses: chunk_sent(remaining)/drained/halted/skipped/failed. Deliberate hardening over the verbatim extraction: the finalise-path count queries and the sent_count refresh now THROW on error instead of reading a null count as 0 — chaining makes the finalise check run ~every 2s, and a mis-finalise strands live rows / clobbers sent_count. New worker `/api/webhooks/qstash/host-campaigns` (maxDuration 300, raw-body-first verify, 503/401 split): processes ONE chunk then SELF-CHAINS — publishes the next kick `{ campaignId, link+1 }` with delaySeconds 2 and **NO dedup id** (each chain link is a deliberate distinct message; a dedup id would be swallowed inside QStash's dedup window since the body is otherwise identical) — bounded by MAX_CHAIN 40 links/lineage (≈2000 rows) after which the cron takes over; chain-publish failure/crash also falls back to the cron (queue table = delivery guarantee). 200 for chunk_sent/drained/halted/skipped; 500 only for infra errors — retry-safe: a crashed attempt's claimed rows are NEVER re-sent (the cron's stale sweep takes them terminal 'failed' — no attempts column). Cron refactored onto the lib as sweeper (header updated): ≤5-campaigns-per-tick loop, stale-claim sweep (now runs BEFORE the chunk and regardless of verify state — crash debris is unsendable either way; the only behaviour delta) and heartbeat stay CRON-ONLY; vercel.json + heartbeat untouched. Kill switch: unset `QSTASH_TOKEN`. openapi.js registered (`{ campaignId: uuidLike, link? }`); INTEGRATIONS.md gains the campaign-kick/chaining paragraph + table row. 67 new tests across lib contract (claim CAS shape, kill switch, consent re-gate, finalisation incl. count-error hardening), worker semantics (chain shape/cap/no-dedup, status mapping), send-route kick (exact shape, dash-only, ordering, every no-publish path) and the previously untested cron (guard, sweep-before-chunk, summary), TDD'd red-first. No migration. |
| 345 | QSTASH.7 — QStash push delivery for class booking requests | Fifth job onto the QSTASH.1 push pattern (plain publish — bookings arrive one at a time, no parallelism bound). Enqueue sites (repo-sweep verified as the ONLY two that insert `class_booking_requests` rows with status 'queued'): `/api/public/class-booking` and the WhatsApp Flow completion handler (`src/lib/whatsapp-flow/completion.js`) — both inserts now `.select('id')` and fire-and-forget `publishQueuePush({ id })` (dedup `class-booking-<id>`, dash-only, no delay; the 23505 dedupe path has no id → no publish, cron covers it). NOT enqueue sites: `/api/admin/backfill-class-bookings` touches the `class_bookings` ROSTER table (different table — nothing to publish); `/api/agent/membership-requests/[id]` only flips rows to booked/failed; the ads libs only read. New shared lib `src/lib/class-booking-queue.js` extracts the cron's per-row logic verbatim: claim CAS (status queued→processing + attempts bump in ONE conditional update, keyed by id) → `processClassBookingRequest` (passed the row AS FETCHED, pre-claim attempts) → the cron's throw-path bookkeeping (re-queue under MAX_ATTEMPTS 3, else needs_review, guarded on status still 'processing'). New worker `/api/webhooks/qstash/class-bookings` (maxDuration 300, raw-body-first verify, 503/401 split): **decision-tree RETURNS are 200 whatever the outcome** — booked/needs_review/failed rows are stamped by the processor itself, a QStash retry cannot improve them; **500 only when the processor THROWS** — the row was re-queued under the cap so the retry re-runs it (the cron's later-tick retry, just sooner); at the cap it went needs_review and the retry 200-skips. Worker eligibility fetch: status 'queued' + attempts < 3 (belt-and-braces; the cron stays the no-filter catch-all). Cron header → sweeper status; reaper stays CRON-ONLY; vercel.json + heartbeat untouched (kill switch: unset `QSTASH_TOKEN`). openapi.js registered; INTEGRATIONS.md table row added. Tests: new lib contract (claim shape, attempts bump, terminal-vs-throw split, bookkeeping guard) + worker response semantics + completion-handler publish hooks. |
| 344 | QSTASH.6 — QStash queued push (parallelism 2) for bulk invoice analysis | Fourth job onto the QSTASH.1 push pattern, and the first with **bounded parallelism**: Claude Vision OCR is rate-limited by design, so instead of plain `/v2/publish/` the enqueue goes through a named QStash FIFO QUEUE (`/v2/enqueue/invoice-analysis/<destination>` — endpoint verified against the @upstash/qstash 2.11.2 source) with **parallelism 2**: a 50-invoice bulk queue becomes at most 2 concurrent worker invocations, never 50. `publishQueuePush` gains additive `queueName`/`queueParallelism` options (existing three callers byte-identical) plus new exported `ensureQueue(name, parallelism)` (`POST /v2/queues` upsert, same env-gated/never-throws/3s-cap contract); the queue is upserted LAZILY — only a 404 on the enqueue triggers upsert + exactly ONE retry (fresh abort signal per attempt). CAVEAT (documented in INTEGRATIONS.md): whether QStash 404s or auto-creates a missing queue is undocumented — auto-create would land at parallelism 1 (MORE bounded, never less), so setup pins parallelism 2 via a one-off `ensureQueue`. Enqueue site: `/api/invoices-inbox/bulk-queue-analysis` is the ONLY place that sets `analysis_queued_at` (repo-sweep verified — bulk-analyse and the cron only clear it); it now allSettled-publishes `{ id }` per queued row after the row updates (dedup `invoice-analysis-<id>`, dash-only) with zero effect on the operator response. New worker `/api/webhooks/qstash/invoice-analysis` (signature verify + 503/401/400/200/500 contract mirrored, `maxDuration` 300) re-fetches the row (still-queued only: `quality_approved` + `analysis_queued_at` set) and goes through new shared `src/lib/invoice-analysis-queue.js`. Claim mechanism: NO new migration — a by-id conditional UPDATE mirrors the `claim_invoice_analysis_batch` RPC predicate exactly (status + queued + claim NULL-or->10-min-stale → fresh `analysis_claimed_at`); under READ COMMITTED the CAS loser re-evaluates against the winner's committed claim → 0 rows → skip, and the RPC's FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED skips worker-held rows, so claim-exactly-once holds in both directions (SKIP LOCKED only ever mattered for the cron's BATCH selection, which keeps the RPC unchanged; the RPC's stale-claim arm remains the crash recovery for both consumers). **Deliberate status-contract difference from the previous workers:** a deterministic extraction failure returns **200 processed**, not 500 — the row is already stamped with its `extraction_error` and DE-QUEUED (INV-BULK.1 design: bad files never retry forever; the operator retries from the UI), so a 500 would just make QStash burn rate limit re-delivering a failure that cannot succeed; 500 is reserved for infrastructure errors (row fetch) where a retry helps. Cron refactored onto the lib's `processInvoiceAnalysisRow` (per-row logic extracted verbatim; RPC batch claim, heartbeat, `vercel.json` all untouched; header → sweeper status). Registered in openapi.js (`{ id: uuidLike }`); INTEGRATIONS.md §QStash gains the queue/parallelism/`ensureQueue` docs + table row. 45 new tests (enqueue endpoint + lazy-upsert retry-once semantics incl. never-throws, `ensureQueue` contract, claim-CAS predicate + de-queue-on-failure semantics, worker status mapping incl. the 200-on-extraction-failure contract, bulk-route publish hook: exact shape, dash-only dedup, no-publish-on-skip/fail, rejection isolation), TDD'd red-first. No migration. |
| 343 | QSTASH.4 — QStash push delivery for the contact-imports queue | Third job onto the QSTASH.1 push pattern: the `*/2` cron stops being the only consumer of `contact_imports`. The commit route's async path (`/api/contacts/import/commit`, >1000 rows) still inserts the `status='pending'` row with its stored payload, then fire-and-forget publishes `{ id }` to QStash with dedup `contact-import-<id>` (dash-only, the QSTASH.2 lesson; no delay — the import should start as soon as QStash delivers). That insert is the ONLY site where a row organically enters 'pending' (verified by sweep: the list/detail/error-csv routes are read-only, rollback only writes `rolling_back`/`rolled_back`, no retry/requeue action exists); the cron's stuck-recovery reset also re-enters 'pending' but deliberately does NOT publish — the cron already claims the oldest pending job in the same tick, and the dedup id would collide inside QStash's dedup window anyway. QStash POSTs new worker `/api/webhooks/qstash/contact-imports` (signature verify + 503/401/200/500 contract mirrored from the previous two workers, `maxDuration 300` — the same budget as the cron, imports legitimately run minutes), which re-fetches the row (`status='pending'` only) and goes through new shared `src/lib/contact-import-queue.js` (`claimAndProcessImportJob`) — claim CAS flips status `pending`→`processing` keyed by id (the CAS the cron always used), then runs `runImportCommit` and stamps completed/failed exactly as the cron did (per-job logic extracted verbatim; NO release-on-failure — a failed import is stamped `failed` for the operator, imports are not blindly retryable). Cron refactored onto the lib (select-oldest-then-CAS replaces the old update-with-order-limit claim; new `skipped` stat for a lost race; missing-payload 500-without-heartbeat branch preserved via the lib's `missingPayload` flag; heartbeat + `vercel.json` untouched). Registered in openapi.js (`{ id: uuidLike }` — contact_imports ids are uuids, unlike the bigint ids of the previous two jobs); INTEGRATIONS.md §QStash table now lists all three migrated jobs. 27 new tests (claim/stamp semantics incl. error-truncation, worker status mapping + maxDuration, commit-route publish hook: exact publish shape, dash-only dedup, publish-rejection isolation, sync path never publishes), TDD'd red-first. No migration. |

Shared `whatsapp-numbers-shape.js` `publicShape()` keeps `access_token`/`signup_meta` (PIN) off the wire — test-asserted. | 328 | SEQ-TERMINAL.1 — terminal branch arms end the enrolment (fall-through fix) | **Prod bug (Anita class):** the runner advanced `step_order + 1` after every non-branch step and the graph compiler emitted no end marker, so a branch arm that terminates fell through into the other arm — "New Lead – First Class Booking Nudge" (seq `21983d6c`) tagged already-booked leads `first_booking_already_made` then sent them the `book_first_visit` template anyway (all 6 ever tagged fell through; 4 got the send). Compiler now stamps every non-branch step's real successor in `config.next_step_order` (`'end'` for leaf nodes, integer for jumps — which also fixes convergent arms silently executing the other arm's rows); the runner completes on `'end'`, jumps on integer, keeps legacy `+1` only for markerless linear-editor rows, and refuses a corrupt self-pointing marker BEFORE dispatch (no repeat sends). Decompile honours/strips the marker so round-trips stay faithful. **Prod rows compiled pre-fix still need healing:** `node scripts/backfill-sequence-step-markers.mjs` (dry-run default, `--apply` to write; idempotent, skips graph-diverged sequences) — run with prod env after merge, then exit any still-active enrolments already mid-fall-through on 21983d6c. +tests: compiler stamping (terminal/convergent/stale-override), runner advance (end/jump/legacy/branch/self-pointer), decompile markers, marker backfill helper. |

| 321 | TAGFIX.1 — Free-text tag audience filter: array-safe operators (#887, merged 2026-07-10) | `contacts.tags` is `text[]`, but `applyAudienceFilter` ran the scalar text path against it (`.eq`/`.ilike`) — a PostgREST 400 on an array column, so **every "Free-text tag" filter had failed since birth** ("Couldn't compute recipient count: HTTP 400", 0 matches; operator-reported 2026-07-10 targeting the PTC import batch). Fix: `AUDIENCE_FIELDS.tags` gains `array: true` → new `applyArrayFieldOp` routes `eq`/`contains` to `.contains(field, [v])` (PostgREST `cs`, element membership), `neq`/`not_contains` to `.not(field, 'cs', '{"v"}')` (quoted/escaped literal); `toOrCondition` gets the matching array branch for OR logic. Semantics: `contains` on the array field now means "has this exact tag" (per-element substring isn't expressible in a PostgREST filter). +8 tests (AND + OR + escaping). **Related discovery — two parallel tag stores that don't sync:** the CSV import batch-tag writes only `contacts.tags`, while the "Segment tag" filter + sequence `tag_added` triggers read `contact_tags` — a fresh import batch was untargetable by either filter until backfilled into `contact_tags` (done manually for the PTC batch; direct INSERT is the sanctioned no-sequences path per `contact-tags.js`). No mig, no schema change. |
| 320 | FREQ-CAP.1 — cross-channel marketing frequency cap (final 2026-07-10 comms audit missing-features item) | Per-location, operator-controlled cap on MARKETING touches across channels: when enabled, one contact gets at most one marketing message — email campaign, WA blast, WA drip, or sequence email/WhatsApp step — per `min_hours_between` window (1–168h, zod-bounded). Setting = `locations.settings.comms_frequency_cap { enabled:false, min_hours_between:24 }`, **OFF by default** (enabling silently would block sends operators expect); card on Settings → Locations → Details + GET/PUT `/api/locations/[id]/comms-frequency-cap` (owner/master write). State = `contacts.last_marketing_touch_at` — **mig 399 (written, NOT applied)** — stamped in batch on every successful marketing send EVEN WHILE DISABLED (history for a later enable); transactional/administrative sends (outbound-stream campaigns, reminders, inbox 1:1, Mia) never stamp. Enforcement (only when enabled, always AFTER consent/hygiene gates): **campaigns** — the chunk fetch adds an embedded-contact `.or(is.null,lt.cutoff)` filter (contacts!inner) so capped recipients stay 'queued' untouched (no attempts bump) and send on a later tick; all-remaining-capped leaves the campaign 'sending' (rotated to the back of the pick order like ab_waiting) with a 7-day safety valve marking still-held rows `skipped_frequency_cap` (free-TEXT status; stats source is email_sends) so a perma-capped contact can't hold a campaign open; **WA blast** — capped contacts excluded from the pass with NO recipient row (resume/re-send re-evaluates them; count surfaced as `delivery_summary.frequency_capped`; budget preflight still sees the full pending set); **WA drip** — `selectDripRecipients` gained an `isEligible` predicate; deferred contacts block `exhausted` so the drip stays open ('awaiting_frequency_cap') and later ticks re-pick them — chosen over the 131049 'capped'-park because it reuses the done-ids machinery, writes no fake recipient rows, and honours the operator window rather than CAPPED_RETRY_HOURS; **sequences** — email+WA handlers throw `FrequencyCapDeferral` BEFORE any provider call; the scheduler catches it and pushes `next_step_at` to window-clear + ≤5min jitter WITHOUT touching error_count/status/cursor (deferred, never skipped, never pause-wedged; nothing sent ⇒ no re-send loop on any path). Pure decision lib `src/lib/frequency-cap.js` (fail-open normalisation, `isFrequencyCapped`, defer/cutoff helpers, batch `stampMarketingTouch`) — 20 unit tests + 9 campaign-tick, 5 drip-selection, 9 step, 3 scheduler tests. SMS sequence steps deliberately not gated/stamped in this slice. |
| 319 | CAMPAIGN-AB — subject-line A/B testing v1 for email campaigns (2026-07-10 comms audit missing-features list) | Classic split test: variant A (`campaigns.subject`) and B (`ab_subject_b`) go to a deterministic test slice (`ab_test_pct`%, default 10, half A / half B, hash-ordered assignment stamped on `campaign_recipients.ab_variant` at populate time), the sender waits `ab_wait_hours` (default 4) after the slice drains, auto-picks the winner by open rate from `email_sends` (ties/zero-data → A), then the remainder sends with the winning subject through the normal chunked path. **Mig 398** (written, NOT applied): `campaigns.ab_subject_b/ab_test_pct/ab_wait_hours/ab_winner/ab_test_started_at/ab_decided_at` (CHECK-bounded), `campaign_recipients.ab_variant`, `campaign_ab_variant_stats(uuid)` RPC (EXISTS-join so retry double-sends can't double-count). Phase machine is column-driven — no new `campaigns.status` values — with every transition CAS'd against overlapping cron ticks (`ab_test_started_at IS NULL` stamp, `ab_winner IS NULL` single-decider); waiting ticks no-op and touch `updated_at` so a waiting campaign rotates to the back of the cron's `MAX_CAMPAIGNS_PER_TICK` pick order instead of starving other sends. Audiences too small to test (<3) short-circuit to winner A at populate. Cancel mid-test unchanged (checked before any phase work). Non-A/B campaigns are regression-locked to the old path. Pure helpers TDD'd in `src/lib/campaign-ab.js` (28 tests) + 12 sender phase tests; editor toggle in CampaignEditor Settings, per-variant panel on CampaignDetail; zod bounds (pct 5–50, wait 1–24h) on `/api/campaigns` create/update. `email_sends.subject` now logs the subject each recipient actually received. |

| 312 | COMMS-AUDIT WA-QUALITY batch — auto-pause drips on quality collapse, blast preflight gate, blast circuit breaker, 131049 classification | PR #875. `applyNumberEvent` now stamps `paused_at` on in-flight drips when Meta FLAGs the number RED or downgrades the messaging tier (idempotent; manager push says what was paused). `sendBroadcast` refuses to start/resume a blast from a RED/FLAGGED number (`force:true` operator override; config resolved BEFORE the draft→sending CAS so refusals no longer strand rows at 'sending'). Blast loop gains the drip's 5-consecutive-failure circuit breaker — on trip the broadcast flips back to 'draft' with `delivery_summary.aborted` + `paused_at` + manager page, so pressing Send resumes the untouched remainder (claim-first inserts skip recorded recipients). 131049 frequency-capped blast recipients are recorded `status='capped'` (never undeliverable, never counted toward the breaker or `total_failed`). |
| 311 | CAMPAIGN-REL — campaign send reliability (COMMS-AUDIT 2026-07-10 email batch) | Four fixes to the campaign send path (`src/lib/campaign-sender.js` + `src/lib/postmark.js`). **(1) Transient-error retry**: `tickCampaignSend` previously marked ANY non-zero Postmark ErrorCode as `status='bounced'` — including the synthetic `-1` sendBatch emits when the whole batch HTTP call fails, so one network blip permanently "bounced" a 500-recipient chunk. New `isTransientSendError()` in postmark.js classifies transient (network `-1`, HTTP 429/5xx via new `HttpStatus` field on synthetic batch results, Postmark codes 100 maintenance / 429 rate-limit) vs permanent (300 invalid email, 406 inactive recipient, …); transient → back to `'queued'` with `attempts+1`, capped at `MAX_SEND_ATTEMPTS` (3) then `'failed'`; permanent still bounces immediately. **(2) Stuck-`sending` reclaim**: rows orphaned in `'sending'` (cron died between the CAS claim and result application) are swept back to `'queued'`/`'failed'` at tick start once their `claimed_at` lease (10 min, mirrors the sequences scheduler `CLAIM_LEASE_MS` pattern) expires — `claimed_at IS NULL` strays included — so finalisation can no longer close a campaign as `'sent'` around them. **Mig 392** (`392_campaign_recipient_attempts.sql`) adds `campaign_recipients.attempts`/`claimed_at`/`last_error`; the new `'failed'` status can't corrupt stats (`recalculate_campaign_stats` counts from `email_sends`, mig 157). **(3) `campaigns.preview_text` wired**: collected by the editor since mig 005 but never used at send time — now merge-tagged and injected as a standard hidden preheader (HTML-escaped, `mso-hide` belt-and-braces CSS, first thing inside `<body>`) via `injectPreheader()`. **(4) Plain-text alternative**: `sendEmail`/`sendBatch` sent HTML-only (a spam signal) — new conservative `htmlToPlainText()` (no new deps; strips tags, keeps `label (href)` link targets, block tags → newlines) derives `TextBody` when the caller doesn't supply one; the campaign path derives it pre-preheader so inbox chrome stays out of the text part. New helpers live in `src/lib/email-content.js` (own module to keep postmark.js surgical — parallel workstream owns `sequences/steps.js`). New `campaign-sender.test.js` (chainable Supabase fake, 10 tests) + extended `postmark.test.js` + `email-content.test.js`. Full CI mirror + `next build` green. |
| 310 | COMMS-AUDIT SEQ batch — sequence step hardening (send-time consent gates, WA location routing, graceful skips, marketing emails → broadcast stream) | 2026-07-10 comms audit, sequences engine (`src/lib/sequences/steps.js` + `src/lib/postmark.js`). **(1) WhatsApp send-time consent gate**: `sendWhatsappStep` now mirrors the broadcast reachability predicate (`applyWhatsAppReachability`, mig 422 denormalised fields) — requires `whatsapp_marketing===true` and refuses `wa_status` in opted_out/blocked/undeliverable — so a contact who texts STOP mid-sequence never receives another WA step. **(2) WA location routing**: `sendTemplateMessage` now gets `{ locationId: sequence.location_id }` so sequence sends route from the location's `whatsapp_numbers` row instead of falling back to env vars (same as broadcasts). **(3) Graceful skips**: per-CONTACT conditions (no `wa_phone`, no consent, blocked channel status) are now recorded skips — best-effort `activities` timeline entry (`recordStepSkip`), handler resolves `null`, scheduler advances the cursor exactly once through its normal path — instead of throws that hit the MAX_ERRORS auto-pause (live: 11 of 17 "New Lead – First Class Booking Nudge" enrollments wedged on missing wa_phone; already-paused rows still need a one-time manual resume). Sequence-CONFIG faults (missing/unapproved template, no body) still throw. **(4) Sequence emails are marketing mail**: `sendEmailStep` gained the campaign-parity consent gate (`email_marketing===true`, matching campaign-sender's `consentOk`; bounced/complained/unsubscribed → recorded skip, no longer an enrollment-pausing throw) and sends via new **`sendMarketingEmail`** (postmark.js) on the **broadcast** stream — which is the only path that attaches the RFC 8058 List-Unsubscribe/List-Unsubscribe-Post one-click headers — logging `email_sends` with `postmark_stream='broadcast'` + the same atomic sequence attribution. Unsubscribe footer append unchanged. Follow-ups flagged: `sendSmsStep` still throws (not skips) on missing phone/opted-out; missing email still throws. Tests in steps.test.js + postmark.test.js; full CI mirror + `next build` green. |
| 309 | FUNNEL.4 — "Cold" button: remove a lead from the pipeline (auto-rejoins if they train) | Operator request (Richard, 2026-07-04): a button to take a lead off the pipeline — reserved for someone not worth selling to or who said they're not interested — that auto-reverses if they come back in and train. Mechanism mirrors converted_at / pack_customer_at: new `contacts.pipeline_dismissed_at` (mig 391 — applied to prod under history name `370_pipeline_cold_dismiss`; repo file renumbered 370→391 to clear a collision with `370_receipt_hunt.sql` on main) is a durable operator-set timestamp the classifier reads. New off-funnel stage `cold_lead` (label **"Cold"**, mig 391, distinct from automatic `dormant`). Classifier (`src/lib/pipeline-classifier.js`) returns `cold_lead` while dismissed AND no attendance has landed since — so `last_attended_at > pipeline_dismissed_at` (came back and trained) auto-revives them into the funnel with no un-stamping; membership/pack/classpass all outrank the dismissal (a cold lead who converts or buys a pack still classifies right). The **one manual pipeline control** on the otherwise-derived board: `POST /api/contacts/[id]/pipeline-status {cold:boolean}` (session + `pipeline` perm + in-location guard) sets/clears the stamp then calls `ensureDealForContact` to move the deal immediately. Button lives in the shared `PersonActionBar` (opt-in `cold` action, dynamic "Mark as Cold" / "Return to pipeline" from `isCold`) → surfaces on the kanban DealCard kebab AND the contact-detail header. Signal parity kept across all three classifier call sites (reclassify SELECT_COLS + snapshot, glofox-sync SELECT_COLS + apply + preview snapshots). Surfaces: 7 display maps, AudienceBuilder/ContactsView/SequenceSettings slug lists, assistant prompt, REFERENCE.md. +7 classifier tests (39 total) + route test. No backfill (nobody dismissed yet). |
| 308 | GLOFOX-NOTES — two-way member-note sync (Glofox ↔ CRM) | Glofox interactions (notes/calls/emails logged front-desk side) already synced INBOUND into the CRM contact timeline via `syncGlofoxInteractions` (`mapGlofoxInteraction`). This branch adds the missing OUTBOUND half plus a provenance marker, notes-only. **(1) Inbound provenance chip**: the unified contact Activity/Notes timeline now tags Glofox-sourced entries (`item.source === 'glofox'`) with a small "Glofox" chip (`src/app/contacts/[id]/page.js`, light-theme `bg-slate-500/10 text-slate-700` ramp) so staff can tell a front-desk Glofox note from a CRM-authored one at a glance. **(2) Outbound push**: adding a note on a contact now copies it into Glofox as a NOTE interaction, fire-and-forget. New session-authed `POST /api/contacts/[id]/notes` (registered in `src/lib/openapi.js`) — `ContactActions.jsx` now posts here instead of writing `notes` directly — inserts the CRM row then, un-awaited, calls `pushNoteToGlofox` (`src/lib/glofox-note-push.js`). The existing API-key `/api/notes` bulk/import path deliberately does **not** push (would mass-spam Glofox with thousands of imported history notes); only the session route carries an author name to attribute. Push is skipped (not an error) for contacts with no `glofox_member_id` or no Glofox creds for their location — non-Glofox locations (CCF Autos, SourceIt) never hit the API. **(3) Glofox API constraints drove the design**: `createGlofoxInteraction` (`src/lib/glofox.js`, `POST /2.1/branches/{branchId}/leads/{userId}/interactions`) is create-only — no update/delete/webhook — so edits and deletes are **never** synced, and the description has no author field, hence `buildInteractionDescription` (`src/lib/glofox-notes.js`) prefixes `[UN1T CRM · <name>] ` and truncates to Glofox's 500-char limit (ellipsis-marked). **(4) Echo suppression**: the Glofox create response carries no id to key off, so every push is logged to a new ledger, **`glofox_note_pushes`** (**mig 390**, `supabase/migrations/390_glofox_note_pushes.sql` — applied to prod via Supabase MCP; RLS-enabled-no-policy, service-role-only like `glofox_sync_runs`; unique index on `(source_table, source_id)` so a note pushes exactly once). `syncGlofoxInteractions` (`src/lib/glofox-sync.js`) now loads each contact's unreconciled push rows before mapping inbound interactions, fingerprint-matches (`matchesPush` — exact description + type + contact, `_id`-less Glofox `created` timestamp within a 2h window of `pushed_at`) each incoming interaction against them, and on a match UPDATEs the ledger row (`glofox_interaction_id`, `status='reconciled'`) instead of upserting into `activities` — so a note pushed by CRM staff shows exactly once in the timeline, not twice. Already-claimed `glofox_interaction_id`s are skipped on every later sync run. Guards against a resolved-not-thrown supabase-js claim error (checked explicitly so a failed claim doesn't fall through to a duplicate upsert). **Scope decisions (operator-approved, Richard 2026-07-04)**: outbound is **notes-only** — call/email mirroring was dropped because the CRM has no completed-touchpoint surface (call/email `activities` are to-do tasks, not logs; pushing a reminder as a member-facing comment would be wrong). Only Glofox-linked contacts push. Full CI mirror + `next build` green. |

GET `event-payments/[id]` now surfaces `connected_account_id` + refreshes Stripe (not just Revolut) so re-entry converges. Operator payment links (copy-link in `RaceTeamsManager`, `payment-sms` route) now point at `/event-pay/[id]` instead of the provider-hosted url (Stripe embedded has none). **Operator action: set `NEXT_PUBLIC_STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` in Vercel.** No migration. Adapter unit-tested (8); full suite + lint + `next build` green. E2E embedded payment needs live Connect test keys + a hosted-by-third-party test event — not browser-verified. |
| 306 | XERO-BILL-VAT.2 — per-location Xero tax-rate sync + rate-accurate bill VAT | Full solution behind the #816 hotfix (which stamped `TaxType` on ACCPAY bills, 0%→NONE, else account default — fixing ROWfit 23967's €156.84 booked as €192.91, but blind to reduced rates). Now each location's Xero tax rates are synced and every bill books under its **derived, confirmed** rate. Spec/plan: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-05-xero-tax-rate-sync-design.md` + `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-05-xero-tax-rate-sync.md`. **New `xero_tax_rates` cache (mig 375, `supabase/migrations/375_xero_tax_rates.sql` — applied to prod via Supabase MCP; advisors clean, `search_path` set on the trigger fn)** mirroring `xero_accounts`: `tax_type`/`name`/`effective_rate`/`status`/`can_apply_to_expenses` per location, stale-row sweep, `xero_connections.tax_rates_last_synced_at/_error` stamps. `pullTaxRates(locationId)` (`src/lib/xero/tax-rates-sync.js`, clone of `accounts-sync`) pulls `/TaxRates` (EffectiveRate, else Σ TaxComponents); folded into the Settings "Refresh" (one button syncs accounts **and** rates, tax-rate failure never undoes accounts) + primed best-effort on Xero connect. Pure `resolveBillTaxType(fields, taxRates)` (`src/lib/invoices-queue/vat.js`): effective rate = `tax_amount ÷ net` (net = subtotal → line-sum → total−tax), **0% → `NONE`** (Richard's call) offering the location's zero-rated/exempt alts, else match to ACTIVE expense-applicable rates within **±0.5pp** → unique `matched` / `ambiguous` / `unmatched`. `GET /api/locations/[id]/xero/tax-rates` serves the picker (registered in openapi). `/invoices` review gains an `XeroTaxRatePicker` (defaults to the derived rate, overridable from the location's real rates; persists `extracted_fields.tax_type` + `tax_type_source`); **`data-approve` is gated on a resolved rate** — a non-zero bill with no `tax_type` returns 400 ("pick a VAT rate"), zero-VAT auto-resolves so it never blocks. `push-xero`'s `resolveLineTaxType` now prefers the confirmed `fields.tax_type` for every line, keeping #816's 0%→NONE + account-cache path as the legacy fallback. Settings card shows tax-rate freshness (needed adding `tax_rates_last_synced_at/_error` to the explicit `xero_connections` select on `settings/locations/[id]/page.js`). Reduced-rate multi-line invoices stay v1-flagged (invoice-level single rate). Built subagent-driven, two-stage review per task; +25 tests (`vat` truth table, `tax-rates-sync` mapping/stale-sweep, push confirmed-type precedence); full CI mirror (6,058 tests) + `next build` green. |

| 303 | AGENT-REARM.2 + PUSH-LOC.1 — Mia never hijacks a human-owned thread; push opt-outs judged per-location | Two live incidents from the Kevin thread (Richard, 2026-07-04, same PR as #302). **(1) AGENT-REARM.2**: the 12h handoff-cooldown re-arm measured from the FIRST handoff stamp (manual sends deliberately preserved it), so 4 days into a human-led exchange the cooldown was long expired and Mia answered a customer who was replying to Richard's own message. Now `sendPush(ids, payload, {locationId})` threads the notification's location through: assignments AT that location gate (falling back to the conservative all-assignments rule for users unassigned there, e.g. master). `sendPushToRolesAtLocation` passes its own locationId; WA webhook + IG `assigned_to` sends pass the conversation's. Ops applied immediately (pre-deploy unblock): explicit `notify_whatsapp/notify_instagram: true` overrides on Richard's SourceIt staff row. 9 new tests (human-owned gate incl. the Kevin timeline; per-location gating incl. | 302 | MIA-REVIEW — conversation-review fixes: follow-ups un-broken, honesty rules, multi-person bookings, 7-day lookahead, handoff SLA | Fixes from Richard's 2026-07-04 full read of Mia's live conversations. **(1) Follow-up engine never fired**: both `runAgentFollowups` and `runFirstClassCheckins` selected the phantom `contacts.opted_out` column — PostgREST 400'd the embed, supabase-js returned `{data:null}` without throwing, so every 15-min tick silently processed zero candidates since launch (the classic verify-schema-vs-live-DB failure; Dean sat one tap from booking for 4 days with no nudge). Now selects the real hard signal `contacts.wa_status='opted_out'` and logs candidate-query errors loudly. **(2) Prompt honesty rules**: never claim to see records not retrieved by a tool (the Priscila "I can see you've been trying to cancel" hallucination staff had to walk back); "Done" only when a tool confirmed execution — queued = "passed to the team"; staff commitments in-thread are acknowledged + handed off, never contradicted; never leak tool params ("how many days ahead should I look?"). **(3) Multi-person bookings**: new prompt section — consultations CAN book each person separately (own name+email per `book_consultation` call); classes book only the verified member, companion details saved + explicit handoff; post-booking questions never re-run availability (the Kevin "5:45pm is now gone" non-sequitur that nearly double-booked a two-person lead). **(4) 7-day lookahead**: `list_upcoming_classes` default days 4→7 AND `MAX_CLASS_LIST` 20→60 (time-sorted truncation meant the model never saw past ~2.5 days — "the schedule only goes up to Thursday" on a Monday). **(5) Handoff SLA** (`src/lib/agent/handoff-sla.js`, rides the agent-followups cron): handed-off threads with no human reply (WA: outbound with `sent_by` set; IG: non-agent outbound) and no resolve within `handoff_sla_minutes` (default 60, 0 disables) re-push managers once per handoff, stamped via new `handoff_escalated_at` on both conversation tables (**mig 369, APPLIED via MCP**; Kevin waited 23h in the black hole). Stillorgan holding message updated in settings to set a response expectation. **(6) Smaller**: `isLikelyBusinessAutoReply` gate (conservative 2-signal match) stops Mia introducing herself to business auto-responders (Zen Movement / PD Aesthetic bot-to-bot); missed-inbound re-run after a reply (bounded ×2) answers messages whose webhook lost the concurrency claim (the dropped "Different time" tap); `MAX_TOOL_ITERATIONS` 4→6; new unverified `request_membership_purchase` tool + prompt section captures "yes to the offer" as a queued `agent_membership_requests` row (kind widened in mig 369) instead of a bare handoff — closes the `eot_salesmsg` "reply yes and I'll sort it" dead-end. Tim's duplicate invite template was confirmed as the already-fixed #747–#749 sequence loop, no new fix needed. 24 new tests; full CI mirror + `next build` green. |

| 297 | INBOX-APPROVALS — Mia approval requests decidable inline in the unified inbox (WA + IG) | Wave 1 of the inline-approvals program (spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-03-inbox-inline-approvals-design.md`; plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-03-inbox-inline-approvals.md`). Approval-request cards now render **inline in the message timeline** at `/communications/inbox` (both WhatsApp and Instagram threads), merged in alongside the normal message stream with realtime updates, instead of only living on the separate `/settings/customer-agent/requests` queue. Decision rights now follow the comms surface rather than a role gate: **mig 363** (`supabase/migrations/363_agent_requests_staff_read_realtime.sql`, renumbered from 357 after the rebase onto main — **must be applied via Supabase MCP before this branch merges**) widens `agent_membership_requests` SELECT to any staff member at the location (needed for browser realtime, previously manager-only), and the `PATCH` decision route drops the `MANAGER_ROLES` gate down to plain location membership — matching who can already see and reply to the thread. To keep that widened write path safe under concurrent staff, the PATCH now takes an **atomic pending-claim** (a conditional update that only succeeds while the row is still `pending`) before executing the Glofox side effect, so two staff deciding the same request at once get one 409 instead of a double-execution. The conversation queue gets a **pending-approval badge** (a batched flag added to both list APIs behind a new partial index for fast lookup, `INBOX-APPROVALS.10b`) so staff can see which threads are waiting without opening them. The `/settings/customer-agent/requests` review page deep-links straight into the relevant conversation. Security hardening landed en route as the surface widened: closed a conversation-existence oracle (uniform 200-empty response instead of a distinguishable 404/empty split), rejected a client-suppliable `actioned` field on the decision payload, fixed a 404 leak on cross-location PATCH attempts, and added a uuid guard. Ruling: **the funnel exists to get new leads across the line to a membership OR a class pack — a pack purchase IS the conversion**, so pack customers are reported in their own off-funnel stage and must never cycle back into the funnel when credits run low. | 293 | FUNNEL.1 — acquisition-funnel pipeline redesign | Operator-approved 2026-07-02: the pipeline is now a pure 5-column acquisition funnel — `new_lead` → `first_class` → `second_class` → `trial_done` → `converted` — plus 3 off-funnel buckets (`member`, `classpass`, `dormant`). Replaces the 9-slug PIPELINE5 taxonomy (`active_trial`/`hot_conversion`/`active_member`/`at_risk_member`/`classpass_active`/`lapsed`/`dormant_classpass` retired). `src/lib/pipeline-classifier.js` rewritten: columns 1-4 key on attendance count from `contacts.recent_bookings` (`last_attended_at` backstop) within 60d windows; column 5 keys on the new `contacts.converted_at`. Hardening fix: a check-in flagged before class start put `last_attended_at` in the future, `daysSince` returned null, and an active trialist got wrongly demoted to dormant — future timestamps now clamp to 0 days ago. **`contacts.converted_at`** (mig 350) is write-once, stamped by `applyMemberSync` (`src/lib/glofox-sync.js`) the instant `glofox_membership_status` transitions into `member`/`credit_member` — webhook path near-instant, nightly sync the catch-all; a returned (not thrown) error from the stamp update is now logged since a failed stamp is permanent (next sync sees member→member, no second chance). **Board is now read-only** (`src/components/KanbanBoard.jsx`, `src/app/pipeline/page.js`): drag-drop removed, columns fully derived, cards show a server-derived next-class badge (from `recent_bookings`, stripped before the client payload) with no-next-class cards sorting first as the follow-up list; tabs renamed Funnel / Off funnel. **Deploy order:** mig 350 (done, live) → classifier + webhook + board code (this branch) → **post-deploy reclassify commit run** → **THEN mig 351** (new file, `supabase/migrations/351_archive_pipeline5_stages.sql`) archives the 7 retired stages, guarded by a `DO $$...RAISE EXCEPTION` that refuses to run while any open deal still points at one — mig 351 also idempotently backfills the off-funnel `dormant` catch-all per location (already applied as ad-hoc DML during the mig 350 rollout for SourceIt + Test Studio, which predated `dormant`; repeated in the migration file so a restored/branched environment converges). **Manual stage-move affordances removed entirely** — they were a lie once placement went classifier-derived (a manual write is silently reverted by the next sync/cron pass): mobile tap-to-move (`mobile/app/pipeline/[dealId].jsx`, `mobile/lib/pipeline-api.js`), the assistant `move_deal` tool (`src/app/api/assistant/chat/route.js`, `src/lib/assistant-prompt.js`), and the sequences `move_pipeline_stage` step type — kept schema-valid in `NODE_TYPES` so legacy drafts don't brick on save, but the executor now no-ops-and-advances (a throwing legacy row must never wedge the runner) and a new `ACTIVE_NODE_TYPES` export is the palette/AI-builder offer-side vocabulary with the retired step dropped; legacy nodes render a greyed "retired" card. Funnel slugs threaded through comms/automations: `AudienceBuilder.jsx` stage options + default filter row, sequence trigger/goal configs (new-member trigger + goal retargeted to `converted`), the sequences AI-builder slug vocabulary, and Mia's check-in cohort narrowed to the four lead columns. Display maps re-keyed to the 8 current slugs: `ContactsTable.jsx`, `PersonHeader.jsx`, mobile `StudioDashboard.jsx` `STATUS_LABEL`, `activity-events.js` timeline labels (with a title-case fallback for any unmapped slug), and the web + mobile studio-dashboard headline tiles (`new_lead`/`first_class`/`trial_done`/`converted` — the old keys would all read zero post-cutover). Two latent bugs found and fixed in the sweep: (1) `src/lib/member-validation.js` — the race member-validation gate was matching literal `pipeline_stage_slug = 'active_member'`, a dead slug under the new taxonomy; now matches `['converted','member']`. (2) `src/lib/hr-session-report.js` — `MEMBER_STAGES` was `['active_member','at_risk_member']`; now `['converted','member']`; the champ-app port of the equivalent logic is unaddressed here and tracked separately. Two data gotchas documented: `contacts.lead_created_at` is import-poisoned (bulk CSV imports stamped it at import time, not lead-acquisition time) so the classifier keys `new_lead` on `joined_at` only, never `lead_created_at`; and `converted_at` is **not backfillable** for historical conversions (Glofox never recorded the moment) — mig 350 seeded it from `joined_at` only for the 60-day launch cohort, older members read as off-funnel `member` with no `converted_at`, which is correct but not retroactively precise. Migs: 350 (`converted_at` + funnel stage rows, applied to prod), 351 (guarded archive, file only — not yet applied). |
| 292 | AGENT-CHECKIN.2 — visibility for the First-class check-in card | Audit prompted by the sequence incidents: the check-in machinery is sound (attendance stamps fresh via real-time webhook sync, all gates pass) but had sent ZERO check-ins ever, and its skips only went to server logs — a silent day was undebuggable from the UI. Now: (1) `runFirstClassCheckins` tallies per-tick skip reasons (`results.reasons` — too_soon/expired/no consent/daily_cap/handed_off/…); (2) the agent-followups cron persists the tick summary via `stampHeartbeat('agent-followups', { followups, checkins })` into the existing `cron_heartbeats.last_outcome` jsonb (no migration); (3) settings GET returns `checkin_stats` (sent today vs cap, all-time, last outcome incl. contact + note, last tick summary); (4) the settings card renders it — "Sent today 0/20 · All time 0 · Last: none yet" + "Last run 21:45 — 0 sent · 3 skipped (too soon ×2, expired ×1)". Placement decision (Richard agreed): check-in STAYS agent-side — its value is conversational (free-form in-window in Mia's voice, [[SKIP]]-if-discussed, replies → next-booking offer / complaint handoff), which the sequence engine can't express; folding it into a flow would downgrade it to a template blast. Natural live test: Tim Ivers attends TROOPER VS Sat 4 Jul 08:00 → check-in expected ~10:00–10:15. |
| 291 | SEQ-LOOP-FIX — WhatsApp/SMS sequence steps re-sent every ~10 min (22P02 silent cursor-advance failure) | Live incident (Tim Ivers got the nudge TWICE, 10 min apart): `sendWhatsappStep` returned **Meta's `wamid.…` string** as its send id, but `sequence_enrollments.last_step_send_id` is a **uuid** column → the runner's cursor-advance update was rejected with 22P02 — and the code never checked that update's error, so no error was stamped, the claim lease (10 min) expired, and the tick re-claimed + RE-SENT. Every WhatsApp sequence step would loop like this (email was safe — it returns the `email_sends` uuid; SMS had the same bug via Twilio's `SM…` sid). Fixes: (1) WhatsApp step returns the inserted `whatsapp_messages` row id; SMS step returns its `activities` row id — handlers must return OUR row uuids, never provider ids (3 guard tests). (2) scheduler now **throws when the cursor-advance update errors** → visible `last_error` + 30-min backoff + pause after MAX_ERRORS, instead of an invisible infinite send loop. Residual (noted): a hard crash in the ~100ms between provider-send and cursor-advance can still double-send once per backoff — rare vs tonight's deterministic loop; a per-step idempotency marker is the future hardening if it ever bites. Tim's stuck enrolment was hand-advanced past the send step mid-incident to stop the loop (2 sends total). |
| 290 | SEQ-FLOW-BTN — sequence WhatsApp steps can send FLOW-button templates (131009 fix) | Live failure: the First Class Booking Nudge's WhatsApp step (template `book_first_visit`, which carries a FLOW button → the in-chat booking Flow) failed with Meta `(#131009) Parameter value is not valid` and sat in 30-min retries. Root cause: `buildTemplateComponents` only auto-attaches the flow-button component (per-contact `flow_token` = `contactId.locationId`) **when the caller passes `opts.locationId`** — broadcasts/drips do, but `sendWhatsappStep` (sequences) passed only `companyName`, so the button component was omitted. One-line fix: pass `locationId: sequence.location_id`. Flow completions round-trip through `/api/whatsapp/flow` exactly like broadcast-sent flows. |

Auto-refreshes every 30s while visible + manual Refresh. New pure `describeNextStep()` in run-history.js (+3 tests). |

| 287 | SEQ-GLOFOX.1 — Glofox new-lead webhook now CREATES the contact + fires contact_created sequences | Diagnosed live (Tim Ivers, Glofox dashboard lead): "New Lead – First Class Booking Nudge" never fired because a brand-new Glofox lead had NO real-time path into the CRM — the webhook only looked up existing contacts (`MEMBER_CREATED` → `contact_not_found` dead end) and the nightly 3am bulk sync deliberately doesn't fire `contact_created` (mass-create guard). | 286 | AGENT-CONTEXT — Mia no longer loses the opening outbound message (broadcast/proactive), so she stops re-asking | Diagnosed from Sam Harley's live thread: Mia replied to his answer as a cold open and re-asked the question the broadcast had already posed. | 281 | COMMS-AUDIT batch 6 — audience OR logic + bounded exclusions + composer/segment first-fire | Final audit batch — four fixes. **(1) Audience OR silently ANDed (MED):** the AudienceBuilder has an explicit AND/**OR** toggle, but `applyAudienceFilter` never read `filter.logic` — it chained `.eq()/.in()/…`, which PostgREST ANDs. So an operator picking "match ANY" got "match ALL" → a far narrower audience than intended. Now builds a single PostgREST `.or()` from the scalar predicates when `logic==='or'` (reserved chars quoted), with the AND path byte-for-byte unchanged. OR combined with tag/event virtual filters (which resolve to AND-combined id-sets) **throws** a clear error rather than silently mislead. **(2) Negative tag/event exclusion = unbounded URL (LOW):** `NOT IN (…)` rode in the GET URL; a popular tag with thousands of members blew the URL-length limit and 500'd. Now combines all negatives into one deduped set and **bounds it (`MAX_EXCLUSION_IDS`)** — over the cap it throws with guidance instead of emitting a broken/truncated query. **(3) Composer SMS scheduling 2-step (LOW):** the composer created a draft then PATCHed it to `scheduled` — if the PATCH failed, a stranded draft. `POST /api/sms/broadcasts` now accepts `status` and creates `scheduled` (with `scheduled_at`) atomically; the PATCH is gone. **(4) segment_added fired for the whole segment on first sync (LOW):** wiring a `segment_added` sequence to an already-populated segment enrolled **everyone** on the next tick (empty snapshot → all members looked "added"). New **mig 333** `contact_segments.memberships_initialized_at`: the first sync establishes the baseline + stamps the marker and **suppresses** the triggers; only later joiners fire. 5022 tests (8 new) + guards + `next build` green; mig 333 applied, advisors clean. |
| 280 | COMMS-AUDIT batch 5 — WA conversation key + manual-inbox routing + webhook sent→failed counters | Four WhatsApp/SMS plumbing fixes. **(1) getOrCreateConversation wrong key (MED):** it looked up/created `whatsapp_conversations` by `(location_id, contact_id)`, but the table's unique key is `(location_id, wa_phone)` (mig 008) and the inbound webhook keys on `wa_phone`. So an outbound (broadcast/sequence) send for a contact whose number already had a webhook-created row could MISS it, then the INSERT hit the `wa_phone` unique constraint → null id → an **orphaned** outbound message not linked to the thread. Rewrote to key on `wa_phone` (matching the constraint + webhook), backfill `contact_id` on an unknown-sender row, and re-read the winner on a race; falls back to `contact_id` only when `wa_phone` is null. **(2) Manual inbox sends omit locationId (MED):** `/whatsapp/conversations/[id]/send` called `sendText/Template/Media` with **no `locationId`**, so a manual reply routed via the env-default number instead of the conversation's location `whatsapp_numbers` row (wrong/agent number, or silent failure on a dead env token). Now passes `{ locationId: conversation.location_id }` to all three. **(3) WA webhook over-counts sent (LOW):** a `sent→failed` status incremented `total_failed` but never removed the message from `total_sent` (counted at blast time). Now decrements `total_sent` on the failed transition, and all counter changes are guarded on a real status change (idempotent against Meta redelivery). **(4) Twilio webhook over-counts sent (LOW):** same flaw — a `sent→failed` Twilio report flipped the recipient row but left `total_sent`/`total_failed` frozen at send-time. Now moves the message OUT of `total_sent` and INTO `total_failed` via the new **mig 332** `increment_sms_broadcast_metric(id, metric, delta)` RPC (mirror of the WA one; applied to prod, advisors clean). 5014 tests + guards + `next build` green. |
| 279 | COMMS-AUDIT batch 4 — sequence runner double-send guard + atomic step attribution | Three sequencing-engine fixes. **(1+2) Runner double-send (MED×2):** `runSequences` had no `SELECT … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (the code comment openly tolerated "two cron invocations grabbing the same row") **and** each step *sends* (email/WA/SMS) **before** the cursor (`current_step_order`) advances — so any re-process (overlapping cron tick, or a crash between send and cursor-update) **re-sent the same step**. The runner now **atomically claims** each due enrolment before processing: a CAS-bump of `next_step_at` to a short lease (`CLAIM_LEASE_MS`, 10 min) under the predicate `status='active' AND next_step_at <= now` — exactly one tick wins, the other re-evaluates against the leased (future) value and skips. The real `next_step_at` is written when processing finishes (or by the error/pause path); a crashed tick re-leases after 10 min. Send-then-advance is kept (so a failed send still retries via `error_count`), but concurrent double-processing — the dominant risk — is now impossible. **(3) Attribution races the webhook (LOW):** `sendEmailStep` wrote `source_type='sequence'`/`sequence_id`/`sequence_step_id` in a **follow-up UPDATE** keyed on `postmark_message_id` — a fast open/click webhook could process the `email_sends` row before the UPDATE landed, so the open was never attributed to the step. `sendTransactionalEmail` now accepts optional attribution params and writes them **atomically with the insert** (backward-compatible defaults — all other callers unchanged). 5014 tests + guards + `next build` green. No migration. |
| 278 | COMMS-AUDIT batch 3 — email send-path correctness (double-send, late consent, webhook drain) | Five email-channel fixes. **(1) Campaign cron double-send (HIGH):** Vercel cron doesn't skip an overlapping invocation, so two ticks could `SELECT` the same `status='queued'` chunk and both call `sendBatch`. Phase 2 now **atomically claims** the chunk (`update queued→sending … WHERE status='queued' RETURNING`) and only sends the rows it won; a concurrent tick re-evaluates the predicate after the row lock releases, matches 0, and claims a different chunk. (`campaign_recipients.status` had no CHECK, so `'sending'` needed no migration.) **(2) No consent re-check (HIGH):** the audience is consent-filtered at *populate* time, which can be many ticks/minutes before a given chunk actually sends. The send phase now **re-applies the exact populate-time gate** (`email_marketing`/`email_administrative` true + `email_status` not bounced/complained) per recipient and parks anyone who unsubscribed/bounced in the meantime as `'cancelled'` (counters come from `email_sends`, so no stat corruption). **(3) Postmark webhook drain double-process (MED):** overlapping drain ticks both processed the same pending rows (double-counting opens/clicks, re-applying bounces). Per-row **CAS claim** (`processed_at NULL→now`); failures roll back to NULL + `attempts++`. **(4) Campaign PUT could force status (MED):** the external-API `PUT /api/campaigns/[id]` applied any `status` and edited in-flight campaigns — could mark a campaign `sent` without sending or reset a `sending` one to `draft` (→ re-populate → double-send). Status enum restricted to `draft`/`scheduled`; edits 409 unless the campaign is draft/scheduled. **(5) Failed Postmark batch treated as success (LOW):** a non-2xx/throwing `sendBatch` returned one error object for the whole 500-email chunk → caller mis-mapped it to email[0] and silently dropped the rest. `sendBatch` now emits one result **per email** so a failed batch is recorded + retried. 5014 tests (3 new) + guards + `next build` green. No migration. |
| 277 | COMMS-AUDIT batch 2 — broadcast send-path atomic claims (no more duplicate sends) | Both SMS and WhatsApp **blast** send loops could send a message **twice** under a duplicate `/send` (double-click, retry, or overlapping pass), because the recipient row was inserted *after* the provider call — so the unique constraint de-duped the *record* but never the *send*. Fixed both with a **claim-first** pattern: insert the recipient row (`status='pending'`) **before** the send, guarded by the unique `(broadcast_id, contact_id)` key, then promote it to `sent`/`failed`. A concurrent pass that already claimed a contact fails the insert and skips — exactly-once even though the done-set was read before the race. **SMS** (`sms.js`): claim-first + the entry status flip is now a **compare-and-swap** (`draft/scheduled→sending`, guarded on the read status; loser bails). This also fixes the medium where a post-send DB write that threw was miscounted as *failed* and crashed the chunk. **WhatsApp** (`whatsapp.js`): the blast had **no entry guard at all** — a second `/send` re-blasted the entire audience. Added a draft/sending guard + CAS flip, a paginated **done-set pre-filter** (`fetchDripDoneContactIds`, resume support), claim-first per recipient, and cumulative `total_sent/total_failed` recomputed from the table (dispatched = sent/delivered/read) so a resumed pass doesn't under-report. **Migration 331** adds the missing `unique (broadcast_id, contact_id)` on `whatsapp_broadcast_recipients` (SMS already had it) — defensive dedup first (0 dupes across 202 prod rows); **applied to prod**. 5011 tests + guards + `next build` green. |
| 276 | COMMS-AUDIT batch 1 — dead `active`-column eradication + WA drip headroom cap-overflow | First batch of fixes from the communications audit (verified findings). **(1) Sequences never advanced (HIGH):** three places still read `email_sequences.active` — a **dead legacy boolean that stays false on every sequence created via `status`** — so the runner skipped active enrolments and the booking trigger never enrolled. Aligned all readers to the live `status` column (the 10+ other call sites already use it): `sequences/scheduler.js:247`, `sequences/triggers.js:180` (booking_created), `churn-radar/action/route.js` (+select `status`), and the dunning picker (`dunning-settings/route.js` select + `ChurnRadar.jsx` showed *every* sequence as "(paused)"). **(2) WA drip could exceed daily_cap (HIGH):** the rolling-24h headroom query (`whatsapp.js`) counted only `status='sent'`, so a delivery webhook promoting sent→delivered→read freed up headroom and the drip over-sent — now counts `DISPATCHED_STATUSES` (the same set the cumulative counter uses), restoring daily_cap as a true ceiling. Pure predicate fixes, no migration. 5011 tests + 6 CI guards + `next build` green. (The dead `active` column is left on disk per the deprecated-column convention; a later migration can drop it.) |
