| 270 | WA-UNDELIVERABLE — fix drip SENT counter, failed-sends box, auto-suppress dead numbers | Three fixes from watching the live "Consultation jun 28th" drip. **(1) Drip SENT counter undercounted** — `sendDripChunk` recounted `total_sent` as `status='sent'` only, so it SHRANK as delivery receipts moved rows to delivered/read (showed 18 when 85 were dispatched). Now counts `DISPATCHED_STATUSES = sent+delivered+read` (monotonic; the blast path was already correct). **(2) Failed-sends summary box** on the broadcast detail (name · number · reason), fed by a dedicated failed-only query (the recipients embed caps at 1000 rows). **(3) Auto-suppress undeliverable numbers** — repeated permanent-looking failures set `contacts.wa_status='undeliverable'`, which `applyWhatsAppReachability` now excludes, and the reachability summary/composer/detail-card gained a "not on WhatsApp" reason. Pure `isUndeliverableError` (Meta code 131026 / "undeliverable" text) + best-effort `markUndeliverableIfPermanent`, wired into the send-time catches + the webhook failed-status path; reversible (an inbound message reactivates). **IMPORTANT — threshold of 2:** 131026 is an *overloaded* code (also fires for transient frequency-capping/throttling), so a SINGLE failure isn't proof a number is dead — suppression requires ≥2 undeliverable failures (`UNDELIVERABLE_FAILURE_THRESHOLD`, standard hard-bounce suppression) so a transiently-throttled engaged contact is never lost. Transient codes (rate-limit 470/471, re-engagement 131047, frequency-cap 131049) never suppress. `mig 327` retroactively flags numbers with ≥2 historical undeliverable failures (0 today — this drip is campaign 1) + refreshes the `wa_status` column doc. 5000 tests (incl. new `isUndeliverableError`/threshold cases) + 6 CI guards + `next build` green; mig 327 applied. Files: `src/lib/whatsapp.js`, `src/app/api/webhooks/whatsapp/route.js`, `src/components/WABroadcastEditor.jsx` (+ `[id]/page.js`), `src/components/communications/UnifiedSendComposer.jsx`. |
| 269 | WA-PHONE-BACKFILL — derive `wa_phone` from `phone` for IE mobiles (Stillorgan) | Follow-up to WA-REACH (#268). `contacts.wa_phone` (the WhatsApp number — a **separate field** from the SMS/`phone` number) was only ever set by an actual WhatsApp interaction (inbound webhook / inbox start-conversation), never derived from the Glofox-imported `phone`. So only **4 of 8,303** Stillorgan contacts were WhatsApp-reachable and broadcasts to imported leads resolved to ~0. **mig 326** backfills `wa_phone` from `phone` for valid Irish mobiles — normalisation mirrors `src/lib/glofox-sync.js#normalizePhone` then strips `+`: accepts `353 8[35679]+7` / `0 8[35679]+7` / `8[35679]+7`; leaves UK (`+44`), IE landlines, foreign, the `+10000000000` placeholder and malformed entries NULL; fills NULLs only (idempotent, Stillorgan-only). **5,905 rows backfilled (4 → 5,909 reachable); 0 malformed written.** The screenshotted `trial` segment went **1 → 3,145 reachable** of 3,650 (505 still excluded = 418 no usable number + 87 no marketing opt-in). Caveats: (a) not every mobile is on WhatsApp — a send to a non-WA number just fails harmlessly; (b) mass-messaging cold imported leads risks the WhatsApp number's quality rating (prefer drip + warmer segments); (c) **no forward mechanism** — new Glofox imports still won't derive `wa_phone` (separate follow-up); (d) amplifies duplicate-contact sends (the 3 "Richard Ivers" rows now share a `wa_phone`) → reinforces the contact-merge follow-up. Pure data migration, applied via MCP; no code/schema change. |
| 268 | WA-REACH — WhatsApp broadcast: truthful pre-send count + exclusion reasons | Fixed the "Sent · 0, no reason" trap. The pre-send recipient count (`/api/communications/audience-count`) was deliberately **channel-agnostic** while the send applied a `whatsapp_marketing` + `wa_phone` + opt-out gate, so the count **overstated reachability** and a 0-recipient send silently flipped to a green "Sent". Confirmed on prod broadcast `790f8b1c…`: the operator targeted a **duplicate** contact (`wa_phone=null`, `whatsapp_marketing=false`) — one of three "Richard Ivers" rows on Stillorgan. **mig 422** denormalises `contacts.whatsapp_marketing` (mirrors mig 155 `email_marketing`; trigger-maintained read-copy, source of truth stays `contact_preferences`; `EXECUTE` revoked from PUBLIC/anon/authenticated to match the email sibling — cleared the advisor WARN) so reachability is **single-table** (no embed → safe `head:true` counts). New pure-ish helper `applyWhatsAppReachability(query)` gates BOTH the send audience (`whatsAppAudienceBase`, now embed-free) and `computeWhatsAppReachabilitySummary` → `{matched, reachable, excluded:{no_number,no_consent,opted_out}}`, shared by the count endpoint (`channel:'whatsapp'`) and the persisted `whatsapp_broadcasts.delivery_summary` (jsonb, mig 422). Composer (`UnifiedSendComposer`) now shows "N match · M reachable on WhatsApp" + an amber exclusion-reasons line and **disables Send when 0 reachable** (the single change that prevents the incident); the send result, Sent list, and broadcast detail explain exclusions. `delivery_summary` computation is **best-effort** (try/catch, null fallback) so a count failure never blocks a send (CLAUDE.md side-effect invariant). Backfill verified (drift 0). Files: `src/lib/whatsapp.js`, `src/app/api/communications/audience-count/route.js`, `src/components/communications/UnifiedSendComposer.jsx`, `src/app/communications/sent/page.js`, `src/components/WABroadcastEditor.jsx`. 4994 tests (9 new) + 6 CI guards + `next build` green; mig 422 applied, advisors clean. NB: the **duplicate-contact merge** and a **wa_phone backfill** for contacts with a valid phone but null `wa_phone` are deliberately out of scope (separate follow-ups). |
| 267 | OWED-PENDING.1 — count PENDING custom-charge fees as owed | Flip-side of #264's over-counting: the radar/profile only counted `glofox_invoices.status='PAST_DUE'`, so a no-show / late-cancel fee sitting **PENDING** in Glofox (applied but not yet collected) was excluded — under-counting what a member owes. (e.g. Claire Foley showed €10 instead of €20: a €10 PAST_DUE + a €10 PENDING No Show Fee. Her separate Dec €20 failed payment predates our Jan-2026 ingestion and is deliberately left out — Richard's call.) Fix: "owed" = **PAST_DUE (any) ∪ PENDING that is a CUSTOM_CHARGE fee** — pending **subscription renewals** (scheduled future payments, not arrears) are explicitly excluded so we don't recreate false positives. New pure helpers `OWED_STATUSES` + `isCountedOwedRow` + `isCustomChargeFee` (`glofox-arrears.js`); applied in `fetchPastDue` + `loadContactArrears` (`churn-radar-data.js`), `aggregatePerson` (`person-aggregate.js`), and the reconcile read (`glofox-reconcile.js`). The reconcile cron is now **status-aware**: a PENDING fee **absent** from the report is KEPT (`pending_open`) — unlike a PAST_DUE row, absence isn't a cancel signal because a pending fee hasn't been charged yet; it still clears when the report shows it settled/forgiven, or via webhook. **Tab routing (Richard's follow-up):** PENDING "awaiting authorization" fees land in the **Unpaid charges** tab ONLY — never the **Overdue** chase-list (a pending charge isn't a confirmed debt to dun). New pure `bucketArrears` (`churn-radar.js`) splits per-contact: PAST_DUE ≥€50 → Overdue, PAST_DUE <€50 + all PENDING fees → Unpaid charges; PAST_DUE-only drives `classifyContact` 'overdue'/the pill (a pending fee never makes a member "overdue"). A contact can appear in BOTH tabs (a real ≥€50 debt in Overdue + a separate pending fee in Unpaid charges). Verified against live data: Claire = €20 in Unpaid charges, €0 on Overdue. un1t-crm only, no migration. 4969 tests (21 new) + 6 CI guards green (`next build` via Vercel). |
| 266 | FIX(hr-metrics) — Apple Health HRV/VO₂max now surface (key alias at ingest) | The champ-app (Pulse) Recovery & fitness card showed nothing for HRV/VO₂max: the app uploads metric keys `hrv`/`vo2max` but the consumer (`shared/wearable-trends-view.js`) reads canonical `heart_rate_variability_sdnn`/`vo2_max`, and the ingest receiver stored them verbatim. Fixed at the receiver (`/api/wearables/apple-health/ingest`) via pure `normalizeHealthMetricKey` (`src/lib/apple-health-body.js`) → aliases applied before the `member_health_metrics` upsert, so it fixes ALL app versions with no OTA. Also renamed 265 existing `hrv` rows → `heart_rate_variability_sdnn` (no `vo2max` rows existed; no collisions) so historical data displays. NB `active_energy` (10,615 rows) is still write-only/dead — flagged for cleanup. 4941 tests + build + route-guards green. (Pulse audit remediation, HIGH.) |
| 265 | P2-7 — pre-class push reminders + engagement→churn analytics | Two estate-audit P2 features. **(A) Pre-class reminders:** a new Vercel cron (`/api/cron/send-class-booking-reminders`, every 5 min) pushes the member a reminder before a booked Glofox class — default **30 min, configurable per location** via `locations.notification_config.categories.class_reminder.lead_times_minutes` (supports multiple leads, e.g. a day-before + final). Reuses `sendCustomerPush` + the `inLeadWindow` math; idempotent per (member, booking, lead) via `customer_engagement_nudges` (new `class_reminder` type, mig 323) keyed `'<booking_id>:<lead>'`. `class_bookings.starts_at` is UTC so the window compares instants directly; only the body's time label uses the location tz (pure `classTimeLabel`/`selectDueClassReminders`/`resolveClassReminderLeadTimes` in `src/lib/class-reminders.js`). Reachability (push-token) filtered **before** dedup so an unreachable contact never burns a dedup row. Mig 323 also seeds the `cron_heartbeats` row. **(B) Engagement→churn analytics:** new **Engagement** dashboard tab (`/dashboard/engagement`, perm `engagement_analytics` — owner+manager+head_coach) cross-tabs the live member base by **friend-count tier** (`member_friendships` accepted) against the churn radar's at-risk % + avg attendance + tenure — the data behind "members with more friends churn less" — and tracks app/social adoption (doubles as an adoption tracker while friend counts are near-zero). Compute-on-read (no new table): pure `summarizeEngagementChurn` (`src/lib/engagement-analytics.js`) reuses `classifyContact`/`scoreMember` for the authoritative verdict; IO `loadEngagementChurn` reuses the radar's `fetchMembers`/`fetchPastDue` (now exported) + a paginated friend-count aggregate. Wired into dashboard `SEGMENTS`, `DASHBOARD_LINK_PERM_KEYS`, `dashboard-redirect`, and `WEB_ONLY_OK` (desktop-only, like the radar dashboards). 4958 tests + 6 CI guards + `next build` green; mig 323 applied, advisors clean (no new lints). ⚠️ Analytics uses raw member fetch (no person-rollup) so totals can differ slightly from the Churn tab's de-duped active base; both reminder + analytics are unverified on a real device / at real friend-adoption. |
| 264 | GLOFOX-RECONCILE.1 — clear stale PAST_DUE fees so the churn Overdue/Unpaid list only shows genuinely-unpaid | The Overdue / Unpaid-charges list was OVER-flagging: it reads `glofox_invoices.status='PAST_DUE'`, a table fed by `INVOICE_UPDATED` webhooks that only ever CREATE late-cancel / no-show fee rows as PAST_DUE — when a fee is later **forgiven / cancelled / written off in Glofox, no webhook flips the local copy**, so members showed as owing money they don't. **Verified against live Glofox** (TransactionsList report, the source `reconcile-arrears` already trusts): Laura Hickey's €35 is `is_forgiven`; Diane Mooney's & John Heenan's fees are simply **absent** from the ledger — all three owe €0. Fix: a reconciliation that treats the report as truth. Pure helpers `indexReportByInvoice` + `reconcileOpenPastDue` (`glofox-arrears.js`): clear a local PAST_DUE row when the report shows it **settled→PAID** / **forgiven→FORGIVEN**, keep it when the report shows it **genuinely unpaid**, and for rows **absent** from the report clear them as **CANCELLED only once older than a 2-day buffer** (a brand-new fee may not have hit the ledger yet) — keeping recent absences. Orchestration `runArrearsReconcile` (`glofox-reconcile.js`) fetches the open rows + report and (on commit) clears them, recording **`reconciled_at` / `reconciled_reason`** (mig 324) for audit/reversibility. Runs as a **daily cron** (`/api/cron/glofox-arrears-reconcile`, 04:00 UTC) + a master **dry-run/commit** route (`/api/glofox/reconcile-fees`). **Dry-run against live Stillorgan data: 47 stale fee rows (€430) clear, 48 real debts (€9,030, incl. €199–€597 memberships) correctly kept** — Diane/Laura/John's fees among the cleared. un1t-crm only, mig 324 (additive). 4948 tests (9 new) + 6 CI guards + `next build` green; advisors clean. NOTE: supersedes the "trust the list" framing in #263 — the list itself over-flags; PR #682 (profile==list) stays HELD until this lands so the false flags aren't propagated to profiles. |
| 263 | PROFILE-ARREARS.1 — contact profile now shows the same arrears the Overdue chase-list flags | Fixed a trust-eroding split: a member (e.g. johnheenan1990@gmail.com — 6 unpaid late-cancel/no-show fees, €55) appeared on the churn-radar Overdue list but their profile showed "nothing outstanding". Root cause was **profile-side under-reporting, not a radar over-flag** (the radar was correct — 6 distinct fees, fresh data, 0 netted): the profile's Arrears metric was computed **only** via the person-group aggregate (`person ? person.arrearsCents : null`), so every **ungrouped** contact (~99%) showed "—"; and the "Payment overdue" pill called `classifyContact(activityContact)` with **no `ctx.pastDueIds`**, so its overdue branch (`churn-radar.js:209`) could never fire (dead for 100% of contacts). New tested helper `loadContactArrears(db, contactId)` (`src/lib/churn-radar-data.js`) mirrors `fetchPastDue`'s exact `glofox_invoices` PAST_DUE/PAID columns + the shared `nettedOutByRetry` settled-retry netting, scoped to one `contact_id` — so the profile, the radar, and the grouped person view all agree. Wired into `src/app/contacts/[id]/page.js`: the ungrouped Arrears card now shows the netted per-contact total, and `classifyContact` is fed `{ pastDueIds }` so the pill lights for members in arrears. Radar/list logic unchanged. Regression test reproduces the John Heenan case (€55, 6 fees, the lone same-amount €5 PAID ~4 months away → 0 netted). **Same-branch follow-up (RADAR-PAY.2):** the radar's per-row "re-pull from Glofox" route (`api/churn-radar/refresh-member/route.js`) had the identical dead-`ctx` bug — it called `classifyContact(fresh \|\| {})` with no `ctx.pastDueIds` AND a `fresh` row lacking `id`, and `paymentTroubleKind` (which it also calls) gates 'overdue' on the same ctx, so a member who owed a fee but whose subscription wasn't separately "slipping" reported `still_flagged: false` and wrongly dropped off. New pure helper `classifyRefreshedMember(fresh, contactId, pastDueCount)` (`churn-radar.js`) injects the id + feeds the netted-arrears ctx to both `classifyContact` and `paymentTroubleKind`; the route now pulls `loadContactArrears(db, contactId).count`. Verified against live data (John: member/active, 6 past-due → now classifies 'overdue'). un1t-crm only, no migration. 4947 tests + 6 CI guards + `next build` green. |
| 262 | TV-KIOSK — kiosk mode for the studio TV (`?kiosk=1`) | Hardens `/tv/[locationId]` as an always-on signage endpoint for a Raspberry Pi 4 + Chromium kiosk (the new Fire TV Sticks block sideloading, so we moved to Pi). Behaviour gated behind `?kiosk=1` so the normal page is unchanged. Kiosk adds: **Screen Wake Lock** (re-acquired on `visibilitychange`) so the display never sleeps; **self-healing reconnect** — the 2 s poll already keeps last-good data on error, now it tracks consecutive failures and shows a quiet corner "● reconnecting…" pill (after 2 misses) instead of the loud red error box, so a gym-wifi blip never blanks the screen; **hidden cursor** (`cursor:none`); **landscape orientation lock**. All browser APIs best-effort/guarded; effects clean up (no leaks for a days-long display). Pure helpers `isKioskParam`/`showReconnecting` (`src/lib/tv-kiosk.js`) unit-tested. (Pairs with the Pi 4 Chromium-kiosk setup guide — separate task.) |

| 260 | AH-BODY (Slice 2, un1t-crm) — Apple Health body-metric ingest receiver | Extends the customer-authed Apple Health ingest (`POST /api/wearables/apple-health/ingest`) to also accept an optional `body` block `{ weight_kg, weight_at?, dob?, biological_sex? }` and apply it to the canonical contact fields from PROFILE-SETUP (#259): **weight → `contacts.weight_kg` via `applyWeightObservation` (freshest-wins)**, and **dob/gender filled only-if-null** (never clobbers a Glofox value or an explicit member choice; legacy `gender='P'` counts as set). | 259 | PROFILE-SETUP (backend) — body metrics → calories for in-studio sessions | In-studio bridge sessions showed "—" for calories because `heart_rate_sessions.calories_kcal` is only ever written by wearable *imports* (Apple Health / Strava) — the bridge captures HR but never estimated calories, and we lacked the inputs (weight, gender). This is the **un1t-crm backend slice** that makes calories compute. **Model (mig 322):** `contacts.weight_kg` (+ `weight_kg_source`/`weight_kg_at` provenance) and `profile_setup_completed_at`. `dob` + `gender` already existed from Glofox — **discovery: `gender` carries a legacy `P` code (~1.5k rows, ≈ "prefer not to say")**, so NO CHECK constraint was added (would reject `P`); new self-service writes are app-validated to female/male/other and the calc treats anything that isn't male/female (incl. `P`/`other`/null) as sex-neutral. **Calorie calc:** pure `estimateCaloriesKcal` (Keytel et al. 2005, HR-based; `src/lib/calories.js`) wired into `endSession` finalisation (so the stale-close cron path inherits it) — best-effort, leaves null when inputs missing. **Freshest-wins weight:** one canonical `contacts.weight_kg` written by whichever source is newest via `applyWeightObservation` (`src/lib/body-metrics.js`); InBody scan ingest now feeds it. **Self-service:** `POST /api/me/body-metrics` (champ-app member, customer-auth via `resolveCustomerContact`, writes only own contact, stamps `profile_setup_completed_at` once dob+gender+weight all set). **Backfill:** master-gated `POST /api/admin/backfill-session-calories` (paginated via `selectAll`, `maxDuration=300`). | 258 | HR-ROUTE — booking-first HR routing + staff test mode + unpaired-strap TV display + class-aware session lifecycle | Fixes a registered chest strap that never reached the studio TV outside a live class, and reshapes how an HR session maps to a class. **Booking-first mapping:** a registered strap now maps to the member's *booked* class (nearest non-cancelled `class_bookings` row, wide 45/30 window) instead of the old time-based "most-recently-started live occurrence" guess — so an early arrival (strap on at 7:45 for an 8:00 booking) lands on their 8:00 class even when a previous class still overlaps. The presence fallback (unbooked member during a live class) stays at the tight −20/+10 window. New pure `pickNearestBookedOccurrence` + IO `resolveBookedOccurrenceForMember` in `class-bookings.js`; `findOrCreateAutoSession` (`bridge-samples.js`) and `pairOverride` (`live-class.js`) both consume it. **Staff test mode:** mig 321 adds `ble_bridges.test_mode_until` (time-boxed, self-expiring); `POST/DELETE /api/live/[locationId]/test-mode` (manager+) flips it; while active a registered strap routes to a presence-less session any time (no class needed) — toggle + countdown banner on `/live/[locationId]`. **Unpaired-strap TV display:** the public TV feed (`/api/public/live`) now lists every broadcasting strap with no open session, shown by a privacy-masked number (ANT+ = device number; BLE = last-4 only, never a full MAC on the public screen) with live BPM — visible immediately, no pairing; pairing/booking upgrades the tile to a named, scoring session. **Class-aware lifecycle:** new pure `hr-session-lifecycle.js` (`classSessionAction`, `shouldCloseStaleSession`); the stale-close cron now *defers* closing a class-linked session while its class is still running (silent + class-not-ended-yet = rejoinable), so a mid-class drop-out (e.g. step out for a run) rejoins the same session on return, and the post-class email fires exactly once at class end (`email_sent_at` is per-row, so one session per member per class is the only safe dedup; class-keyed create/reopen/skip enforces it). All four layers gated behind a confirmed booking or an explicit toggle, so nothing spurious spawns. Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-27-hr-booking-first-routing-design.md`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-06-27-hr-booking-first-routing.md`. 4883 tests + 6 CI guards + `next build` green; `get_advisors` clean after mig 321. ⚠️ Not device-verified — the whole HR path still awaits a real strap through a real class. |
| 257 | STUDIO-PIN — fix iPad stranded on login after a correct PIN (mobile) | On a paired studio device the PIN pad is an overlay above whatever screen is underneath. A correct PIN **mints a real Supabase session** (confirmed: `auth.sessions` rows created) and unmounts the pad — but nothing navigated to `/(tabs)`: `index.jsx` only redirects at route `"/"`, and the PIN path (unlike email sign-in, which calls `router.replace('/(tabs)')`) never navigates. Result: staffer stranded on the login screen holding a valid session, and re-pairing did nothing (the pad won't re-show while a session exists). JS-only → **OTA-deployable** (no native rebuild). Diagnosed from `pin_login_attempts` (3× `success`) + `auth.sessions` (2 rows) proving server-side mint worked while the UI never advanced. Lint + all CI guards + 4861 tests green; device-verify is the operator's final step. |

Anti-enumeration posture unchanged — the truth is only ever exposed to an authenticated master in the admin UI, never to the device. | 255 | PERF — consolidate `multiple_permissive_policies` advisor WARNs (mig 320) | Supabase performance advisor had grown to **91 `multiple_permissive_policies` WARNs** (up as new feature tables landed — `challenges`, `member_health_metrics`, `ble_bridges`, `strap_assignments`, `xero_connections`, `shift_swap_requests`, …). Root cause: the common `FOR ALL "manage"` + separate read-policy pattern overlaps on SELECT, counted ×5 grant roles when left at default `PUBLIC`. One migration across **38 tables** cleared all 91 → **0**, behaviour-preserving by construction: (a) **merge** overlapping SELECT policies into one `USING (q1 OR q2 …)` — RLS already ORs permissive policies, so the visible-row set is identical; (b) **split** each `FOR ALL` manage policy into explicit `INSERT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE` (when WITH CHECK was null, USING copied into the split check). Merged role set = union of contributing roles (never narrows; `private.auth_*()`/`auth.uid()` yield nothing for anon, so widening to public adds no rows). One exception — `google_reviews` (a `{public}` member-read + `{anon}` public-read can't merge) — narrowed the member policy to `{authenticated}` (its predicate already excluded anon). Applied via MCP (remote `20260627113932`); local record = `supabase/migrations/320_*.sql`. **Verified:** advisor 91→0, no new WARNs, security advisors unchanged; RLS impersonation smoke test — anon sees **0 rows on every protected table**, master/staff retain full access (`contacts` 8310 == old union, `profiles`/`member_health_metrics` unchanged). Convention added to CLAUDE.md ("one permissive policy per (table, command)") so new tables stop regenerating the WARN. The 2 remaining SECURITY DEFINER WARNs (`list_enabled_integrations`, `scan_straps_for_contact`) are unchanged — documented intentional exceptions (migs 154/156); revoking would break the customer portal. |

(1) **Phone auto-verify required an EXACTLY-ONE contact match**, so a member whose number sits on several duplicate records (Glofox + ClassPass) was ambiguous → quiz. Now **link-aware**: new pure `resolveAutoVerify` (`agent/core.js`) verifies when every number-match is one linked Person (`person_groups`) incl. the thread contact, and resolves the acting account to the group **primary** via `resolveActingContactId` so bookings/lookups hit the canonical record; a number genuinely shared by two *different* people still stays ambiguous → quiz. Batch `personGroupResolver` (`person-links.js`) feeds the group data (one/two `.in()` SELECTs → `groupOf`/`primaryOf` closures). (2) **A still-fresh prior (quiz) verification was honoured by the tools but never surfaced to the model**, so it re-asked; the "already verified — don't ask" prompt block now also fires for a fresh stored verification (within the existing 30-day `VERIFY_TTL_MS`), wording made provenance-neutral. Wired into `auto-reply.js` (also resolves the quiz path to primary). Channel-agnostic → Instagram gets act-on-primary + sticky-verification for free. No schema/permission/route change. Design: `docs/AGENT_LINK_AWARE_VERIFY_2026-06.md`. 4646 tests (+17, TDD), Vercel build green. |

| 229 | IMPERSONATE-REAP.1 — auto-close stale impersonation sessions (mig 240) | `impersonation_log` rows were closed only by an explicit Stop or a re-target, so a session that ended any other way (tab close, logout, cookie/SecureStore expiry) left the row open **forever** — misrepresenting a short debug session as "still ongoing" in the staff-visible access history (this is the recurring root cause behind the dangling-banner reports; auth.js needs both the cookie AND an open row, so a dangling row alone is inert). Fix: (1) new hourly `/api/cron/close-stale-impersonations` reaper (`closeStaleImpersonations()` in `src/lib/impersonation.js`) stamps a truthful upper-bound `ended_at` = `started_at + session max-age` plus `auto_closed=true` (mig 240 adds the column + heartbeat row) on any row open past the max-age — catches every leak path; (2) session max-age shortened 24h→**2h** via a single shared `IMPERSONATE_SESSION_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` (web cookie + mobile SecureStore `MAX_AGE_MS` + reaper cutoff, kept in lockstep) to bound both abandoned-session exposure and audit imprecision; (3) logout now closes the session immediately on web (`Sidebar` `handleLogout`) and mobile (`auth-context` `signOut`) instead of waiting for the reaper. 2890 tests (6 new: pure close-planner + reaper IO). |
| 228 | PIPELINE-FIX.2 — live-membership floor: paid-up members never fall to dormant/lapsed (PR #287) | Pipeline review follow-up. The classifier kept members `active_member` via `last_payment_at` (paid ≤60d), but that column is populated for only ~5.9% of contacts — written solely by the manual Glofox invoice backfill, never by the recurring member sync — so paying members who don't attend weekly dropped into the hidden `lapsed`/`dormant` buckets. Fix: `glofox_membership_expiry` (future = paid up) is now the authoritative "live membership" floor in the member branch — a paid-up member can only be `active_member` (attended ≤30d or paid ≤60d) or `at_risk_member` (paid up but quiet), never lapsed/dormant. `locked` still overrides to `at_risk`. This also subsumes `paused` handling without a dedicated branch — and avoids demoting 9 paused-but-still-attending members into churn-save sequences. `glofox_membership_expiry` (+ `state`, where missing) wired through all three classifier call sites (reclassify cron object literal + both `glofox-sync` snapshots, which had omitted the lifecycle fields). Live preview: 22 paid-up members move out of the hidden buckets (19 dormant→active_member, 2 dormant→at_risk, 1 lapsed→at_risk); applies on the next nightly `pipeline-classify` run. 2884 tests (8 new floor cases). |

Added the column to both places. (2) mig 239 archived the 9 dead pre-PIPELINE5 legacy stages (`new_lead_social`, `trial_active`, `conversion_ready`, `follow_up_needed`, `member`, `cold_email_only`, `lost_member`, `returning_member`, `credit_member`) — all 0 deals, never written by the classifier — so the Kanban shows the real 9-stage model instead of 18 columns (`pipeline/page.js` filters `archived=false`). `new_lead` kept (shared with the new model). | 210 | REPORT-ISSUE.2 — owner inbox + claim/resolve/close flow + push notifications (branch `report-issue-2`, PR #169) | Handler side of the staff issue reporting feature. Web `/issues` page (owner + master only) with tabs (Open=open+in_progress, Resolved, Closed), status pills, list view of submissions with attachment counts, and a drawer drill-in showing the photo strip (signed-URL fetched via a new handler-scoped endpoint), submitter / claimant / resolver names, and an action footer (Claim / Mark resolved with notes / Close). **State transitions** all in `src/lib/issues.js`: `claimIssue` (open → in_progress, concurrency-safe via `status='open'` predicate), `resolveIssue` (open OR in_progress → resolved, notes mandatory 1..2000 chars), `closeIssue` (resolved → closed, terminal). 409s on race conditions with friendly "refresh" messages. **New routes**: `GET /api/issues/inbox` (status filter), `GET /api/issues/[id]/inbox`, `GET /api/issues/[id]/inbox/attachments/[id]` (signed URL, 10 min TTL), `POST /api/issues/[id]/{claim,resolve,close}`. All audit-logged (`issue.claimed`, `issue.resolved`, `issue.closed`). Existing `POST /api/issues` (REPORT-ISSUE.1) extended to fire `notify_issue_submitted` to owner + master at the location on submit; resolve route fires `notify_issue_resolved` to the submitter with the resolution notes preview. Both pushes via the existing `sendPush` / `sendPushToRolesAtLocation` plumbing — best-effort, never blocks the state change. **New permission key** `issues_inbox` (master + owner default ON, everyone else OFF). **New mobile notify keys** `notify_issue_submitted` (handlers — owner + master default ON) + `notify_issue_resolved` (submitter — default ON for everyone since it's their own report). **/approvals integration** — new `issuesProvider` in `src/lib/approvals/providers/` so open + in_progress issues bubble up alongside expenses and time-off; `isVisible()` self-gates on owner-at-active-location so non-handlers don't see an empty tab. **Sidebar entry** under Approvals with AlertCircle icon, gated on `issues_inbox`. **Tests**: 19 new vitest cases covering every state transition + 409 race paths. 2590 / 2590 tests pass; lint + mobile parity clean. |
| 209 | REPORT-ISSUE.1 — staff issue submission + mobile flow (mig 213, branch `report-issue-1`, PR #164) | First of two for "Report a problem". Universal CTA on the mobile More tab (no permission gate — any staff member can submit). **Schema (mig 213)**: `issues` table (location_id, submitter_id, description, status=open\|in_progress\|resolved\|closed, claimed_by/at, resolved_by/at, resolution_notes, closed_at, timestamps) with indexes on (location, status, created_at desc) for the inbox, on submitter_id for the user's own list, and a partial index on claimed_by. `issue_attachments` table (0..N photos per issue: path, bucket, size, mime, position, uploaded_by) — cascade-deletes with the parent. Dedicated `issue-photos` Supabase storage bucket with belt-and-braces deny-all RLS on `storage.objects` (mirrors the `fte-expense-receipts` pattern from mig 183). RLS service-role-only on both new tables — API mediates. **Lib (`src/lib/issues.js`)**: pure helpers (`slugifyFilename` preserves underscores for iOS Camera IMG_0001 readability, `buildAttachmentPath`, `validateSubmission` with 4000-char desc cap + 3-photo / 10MB-per-photo cap + JPEG/PNG/WebP/HEIC mime gate) plus `insertIssueWithAttachments` (issue id pre-minted so storage paths can be namespaced by issue_id before the row exists; on attachment-insert failure the parent row is rolled back). **API**: `POST /api/issues` multipart with `photo_0..2` keys, `GET /api/issues` for own submissions, `GET /api/issues/[id]` (404s for non-submitters so existence isn't leaked), and `GET /api/issues/[id]/attachments/[id]` for 10-min signed URLs. Audit event `issue.submitted`. **Mobile**: "Report" section on the More tab with `/issues/new` modal form (camera + library picker, up to 3 photos), `/issues` history list with status pills + FAB, `/issues/[id]` read-only drill-in (photos via signed URLs, amber "owners have been notified" banner until PR 2 wires the actual flow). **No permission key** introduced — routing target ("All owners at the studio") is implemented in PR 2 via role IN ('owner','master') at the location. 27 vitest tests pass; lint + mobile parity clean. |
| 208 | hr-analytics flake hotfix — thread nowMs through pickHighlight (branch `hr-analytics-test-fix`, PR #168) | Pre-existing time-sensitive test failure that was blocking CI on PR #167. `pickHighlight` in `src/lib/hr-analytics.js` was the only function in the file that didn't accept a `nowMs` param — its internal `withinDays(historyExclThis, RECENT_DAYS)` call defaulted to real `Date.now()`. The test fixtures were dated relative to `NOW = 2026-05-08`, so once today's real date moved more than 28 days past that anchor, the "last 28d" window started returning an empty sample, the percentile check returned `null`, and the `top_quartile_recent` assertion blew up with "Cannot read properties of null (reading 'id')". **Fix**: add `nowMs = Date.now()` param to `pickHighlight`, forward it to the internal `withinDays`, and have `buildSessionAnalytics` thread its own `nowMs` through to `pickHighlight`. All `pickHighlight` tests now pass `nowMs: NOW` so they're insulated from future calendar drift. No behaviour change for production callers (they continue to default to real `Date.now()`). 2552 / 2552 tests pass after the fix. |
| 207 | CHECKLIST.1+2+3 — role-and-day checklists with shift-anchored coach view + deadline-sweep cron (migs 214 + 215, branches `checklist-1` / `checklist-2` / `checklist-3`, PRs #165, #166, #167) | Three-PR feature for staff accountability — each shift has a per-role / per-day-of-week checklist the coach ticks off before their shift ends. PR 1 ships templates, PR 2 the mobile view + lazy instance generation, PR 3 the deadline cron + notifications. **Schema**: mig 214 adds `checklist_templates` (location_id, role, day_of_week 0..6 with Pg dow convention Sun=0, name, items jsonb, enabled, unique on (location, role, day)). mig 215 adds `checklist_instances` (template_id, profile_id, location_id, date, status=pending\|complete\|incomplete, items jsonb snapshotted from the template at generation time, items_checked jsonb keyed by item id with `{checked_at, checked_by}`, deadline_at, completed_at, unique on (profile, date, template)). Partial index on `deadline_at WHERE status='pending'` powers the cron sweep; secondary index on (location, date desc, status) for the future non-compliance dashboard. RLS service-role-only on both. **Why JSONB snapshot for items**: instance carries its own copy of the template at generation time so a mid-shift template edit (renaming "Wipe kettlebells" → "Sanitise kettlebells") can't shift state under the coach. Item ids are stable so renames preserve tick state. **Lib**: `src/lib/checklists.js` (template CRUD + items validator — caps 50 items, 200-char labels, preserves caller-supplied ids), `src/lib/checklist-instances.js` (lazy resolution: `todayInTimezone` honours `locations.timezone` via Intl.DateTimeFormat with DST + Sydney-cross-the-dateline test coverage; `computeDeadlineAt` returns end of the latest shift block today; `resolveTodayInstances` finds the matching template if the coach has a non-cancelled shift assignment today and upserts the instance; `tickItem` validates ownership + snapshot membership and auto-recomputes status to `complete` when every snapshot item id is keyed). `src/lib/checklist-sweep.js` (pure planner + the concurrency-safe `markIncomplete` that only flips rows still in `'pending'`). **API**: master + owner edit at `GET/POST /api/checklists/templates` + `GET/PATCH/DELETE /api/checklists/templates/[id]`; mobile reads `GET /api/mobile/checklists/today` (lazy creates) and ticks via `POST/DELETE /api/mobile/checklists/[id]/items/[itemId]`. **UI**: `/admin/checklists` (master + owner) renders a 4 (role) × 7 (day) grid (Mon..Sun display) with click-to-edit drawer including ordered item list + add/move/remove inline. New "Checklists" card on the /admin hub. Mobile: `TodayChecklistCard` self-renders null when no instance exists (silent for most users on most days), drilling into `/checklists/today` with tap-to-tick rows, progress bar, optimistic update + server confirm, deadline countdown that flips amber past deadline / green on complete. **Cron** `/api/cron/checklist-sweep` runs every 15 min via vercel.json — finds expired pending instances, flips status to `incomplete`, fires two pushes per row (personal `notify_checklist_overdue` to the coach with the missed-items list, and compliance `notify_checklist_compliance` to head_coach + owner + master at the location with "Alice missed: …"), audit-events `checklist.incomplete`. Per-row error isolation so one bad push never stalls the loop. **Permission keys**: new mobile `notify_checklist_overdue` (default on for every role — staff coaches get the personal heads-up) + `notify_checklist_compliance` (default on for head_coach + manager + owner + master). Both respect the manager-superset-of-staff parity invariant. **Tests**: 66 new vitest cases across the three PRs (template validators, planExternalRule-style truth tables, deadline computation across timezones, tick auto-complete + revert paths, mark-incomplete concurrency, push-body truncation). The hr-analytics flake (#208) was discovered + fixed during the CHECKLIST.3 CI run since CI exercises the full suite. After all three PRs land: 2571 / 2571 tests pass; lint + mobile parity clean. |
| 206 | STUDIO-AC — bathroom AC control end-to-end (migs 210/211/212, branches `mobile-ac-list` + `ac-params-hotfix` + `thinq-api-key-hotfix` + `ac-device-groups` + `ac-external-state` + `thinq-conditional-control` + `studio-ac-external-rule` + `thinq-sequential-posts` + `thinq-temperature-shape`, PRs #155–163) | Multi-PR cluster that added LG ThinQ Connect control alongside the existing Sensibo (gym-floor) and shipped four production hotfixes once the operator started using it. The original three PRs (STUDIO-AC-DEVICES.1/.2/.3) introduced the multi-vendor dispatcher and were already in the previous Done log (#188-#191 area in operator memory); this row captures the iOS catch-up, the Mobile-AC list, the LG hotfix series, and the operator-driven feature follow-ups. **MOBILE-AC.1** (PR #155): adds a fresh `mobile/app/ac/index.jsx` AC device list under the More tab driven by the same allowlist-filtered server endpoints as the web panel; per-card vendor poll + Turn on / Turn off / Extend buttons. **HOTFIX 1 — async params** (PR #156): `withAuth` was passing `ctx.params` straight through to handlers without awaiting (latent since Next.js 15+); the new STUDIO-AC-DEVICES routes were the first withAuth-wrapped consumers using params and surfaced the bug as a "Location id required" error in the StaffForm AC allowlist picker. Fix at the wrapper: `await ctx.params`. **HOTFIX 2 — LG ThinQ API key + headers** (PR #157): hardcoded `x-api-key` was partly hallucinated (32 chars vs the real 40 chars); also need lowercase `x-*` headers (gateway is strict) and `x-service-phase: 'OP'` (undocumented in LG's public API ref, only visible in `pythinqconnect/const.py`). Without these, every call 401s regardless of PAT validity. **STUDIO-AC-GROUPS.1** (mig 211, PR #158): `ac_devices.device_group` text column so the panel renders one section per group ("Gym Floor", "Bathrooms"). Master-editable per device from the AC Devices settings tab; sensible defaults backfilled per provider (sensibo→Gym Floor, thinq→Bathrooms). **STUDIO-AC-EXTERNAL.1** (PR #159): the AcControlPanel now reconciles the live vendor state with the session row — vendor on + no session = `control_source='external'` (running but started outside the CRM, no countdown but Turn-off still works); vendor off + session = stale-session cleanup so the auto-off cron doesn't try to re-stop an already-stopped unit. **HOTFIX 3 — x-conditional-control header** (PR #160): turned out to be a red herring — LG's `/control` rejects composite payloads (more than one resource block in a single body) with code 2207, and the header alone isn't enough. We added it because the Python SDK includes it unconditionally, but the real fix was sequential POSTs (PR #162). **STUDIO-AC-EXTERNAL-RULE.1** (mig 212, PR #161): per-device `external_auto_off_minutes` column (NULL = rule off; backfilled from existing `session_minutes`) + `ac_external_starts` tracking table + new `/api/cron/ac-external-rule` cron at every 5 min that polls every enabled device, upserts the table on observed external start, and turns off via `vendorTurnOff` + audit-logs once `expected_off_at` passes. First observation wins ("hard ceiling" not "sliding window"). Panel + mobile UI now show a real countdown for externally-started units. **HOTFIX 4 — LG sequential POSTs** (PR #162): the actual root cause behind the persistent "Invalid command error" — LG's `/control` silently rejects any body that touches more than one resource block. The official Python SDK never combines; every `set_*` method fires a single-attribute POST via `do_attribute_command` / `do_enum_attribute_command`. `setDeviceState` rewritten to split the composite body from `buildTurnOnState` into N sequential single-resource POSTs in `[operation, airConJobMode, temperatureInUnits, temperature, airFlow]` order (operation first so LG accepts the rest on a now-powered unit). turnOff stays one POST (single resource), no behaviour change. **HOTFIX 5 — temperatureInUnits shape** (PR #163): with sequential POSTs, the first POST powered on the unit but the temperature POST still rejected — turns out LG's AirConditioner profile_map has NO `temperature` resource at all; only `temperatureInUnits` with `targetTemperature` (no C suffix) and a sibling `unit: 'C'`. `buildTurnOnState` now emits `temperatureInUnits: { targetTemperature: t, unit: 'C' }`. Also made non-`operation` POSTs best-effort: if `POWER_ON` succeeds but a downstream resource fails on some firmware (e.g. `windStrengthDetail` vs `windStrength`), we log a warning + carry on so the unit comes on with our defaults partially applied rather than stranding the user with an error while the AC is running. `normaliseState` now reads `temperatureInUnits.targetTemperatureC` first, falls back to legacy `temperature.*` and to `airFlow.windStrengthDetail`. After all five hotfixes and the two operator-driven features land: 116 tests across the AC + thinq + sensibo suites; lint + mobile parity clean. |

| 196 | INVOICES.1 — Dext-style email-in supplier invoice ingest (mig 184, branch `invoices-inbox`, PR #43) | Forward supplier invoices to `<slug>-invoices@mail.un1tdublin.com` (one slug per location, unique partial index, configured in Location settings). Slug resolution falls back through `ToFull → To → OriginalRecipient` so CC'd/BCC'd invoices route correctly. **CRUD + transition routes**: `/api/invoices-inbox` (list, direct upload), `/api/invoices-inbox/[id]` (detail, delete-rejected-only), `/quality-approve`, `/quality-reject`, `/extract`, `/fields` PATCH, `/data-approve`, `/data-reject`, `/attachment` (signed URL, 5min TTL). **Web UI**: `/invoices` page with `InvoicesInbox` — tabbed master/detail with PDF preview alongside editable extracted-fields form. Sidebar entry (Inbox icon) gated by new `invoices_inbox` permission key (master + owner default ON, everyone else OFF, in `WEB_ONLY_OK` for parity linter — desktop workflow because PDF preview alongside fields wants screen real estate). **Per-location slug config**: new "Invoice Forwarding" section in `LocationForm.jsx` with live `<slug>-invoices@mail.un1tdublin.com` preview. **DNS**: MX for `mail.un1tdublin.com` → Postmark's inbound MX (dedicated subdomain keeps marketing apex `un1tdublin.com` free for its own mail). **Env**: `POSTMARK_INBOUND_WEBHOOK_TOKEN` (token used in the URL path; the value is generated via `openssl rand -hex 32` and lives in Vercel env vars only — see operational notes at the bottom of this file). 25 new tests cover address parsing (including +tag, MailboxHash, OriginalRecipient fallback, wrong-domain rejection), status machine transitions (terminal-state absorption, illegal-skip rejection), storage path partitioning, and filename safety. 1953 total tests pass. |

| 192 | CI-FIX.1 — Web CI typescript devDep + PR trigger (branch `ci-fix-typescript-dep`) | Every Web CI run on `main` had been failing for 8 consecutive merges with `Cannot find module 'typescript'` thrown from `eslint-config-next` → `@typescript-eslint/parser` → `@typescript-eslint/typescript-estree`. The transitive plugin chain `require`s `typescript` at load time even on a pure-JS project. Locally my `node_modules/typescript` was hoisted from another resolution so lint passed; CI's clean `npm install` never pulled it. **Fix 1**: add `typescript ^5.9.3` to `devDependencies` (still pure-JS project — just satisfies the plugin loader). **Fix 2**: add `pull_request` trigger to `.github/workflows/web-ci.yml` so future PRs get CI before merge. Previously only `push: branches: [main]` was wired, so we found out about failures only AFTER merging — exactly how this dep gap went undetected for so long. Also added `eslint.config.mjs` to the watched paths (had been missed when the project switched from `.eslintrc.json` to flat config). The streak's broken — first green Web CI in 8 merges. |

Used in three places. **Wizard step 2** (`ContractIssueWizard.jsx`): new "Unmapped variables" amber-bordered section appears under the declared-variables form whenever the template references placeholders that aren't in `variables_schema` and aren't profile-auto-fills. Issue button is disabled with a tooltip ("Fill N remaining placeholder(s) before issuing.") until everything resolves. **API safety net** (`/api/contracts` POST): after rendering the body, `extractPlaceholders(bodyRendered)` runs one more time — if anything remains, the route returns 400 with `unmapped_keys: [...]` so any other client (script, future mobile-issuer, API consumer) can't bypass the check. **Tests**: 5 new cases on `unresolvedPlaceholders` (everything-filled, mix of unfilled, whitespace-as-unfilled, null/undefined-as-unfilled, defensive null-recipient). 1868 tests / lint zero / parity clean / build clean. |
| 190 | POLICIES-VIEWS.1 — replace policy acknowledgement with passive view tracking (mig 179, branch `admin-hub-and-policy-views`) | Operator preference: drop the explicit "I have read and understood" affordance and replace with passive telemetry — record when a policy is opened, how long, and which sections drew the longest attention. **Mig 179**: drops `policy_acknowledgements` (no rows existed — policies were seeded the same day this swap landed) and adds `policy_views` (`id`, `policy_version_id`, `profile_id`, `started_at`, `ended_at`, `total_duration_seconds`, `section_dwell` JSONB `{heading: seconds}`, `viewed_via`, `ip_address`, `user_agent`). RLS: SELECT own or master/owner; INSERT/UPDATE own only; no DELETE. **Lib** (`src/lib/policies.js`) rewritten: `listPoliciesWithStatus` now returns `viewed_at` + `view_count` per policy (computed from the most-recent **completed** view for that user — `ended_at IS NOT NULL`). New helpers: `listVersionViewers(versionId)` (admin report: per-profile session count + total seconds + most-recent timestamp + outstanding-staff split), `sectionDwellAggregate(versionId)` (sums section dwell across all completed views → `{section, total_seconds, avg_seconds, sessions}` sorted desc — drives the "Hot sections" admin panel), and `detectSectionHeadings(body)` (heuristic: numbered headings like "1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE" OR ALL-CAPS lines ≥5 non-whitespace chars, both followed by a blank line). **API**: removed `/api/policies/[slug]/acknowledge`; added `POST /api/policies/[slug]/views` (open a session, returns `view_id`) and `PATCH /api/policies/[slug]/views` (close/progress; idempotent — server takes `max(prev, new)` on duration so visibility-change + pagehide + unmount flushes all converge). **Web viewer**: `AcknowledgePolicyButton.jsx` deleted; new `PolicyViewTracker.jsx` client component splits the body into sections at heading boundaries, mounts an IntersectionObserver per section to detect which one's centred in the viewport (`rootMargin: '0px 0px -50% 0px'` + threshold grid), runs a 1Hz ticker while the tab is visible, and flushes via `fetch(..., { keepalive: true })` on visibilitychange/pagehide/beforeunload/unmount. `sendBeacon` ruled out (POST-only — can't issue PATCH). `flush()` is declared above the `useEffect` that closes over it (avoids the `react-hooks/immutability` lint flagging the function-declaration hoisting). **Mobile viewer**: same approach, native-flavoured — `onLayout` on each section View captures `y + height`, `onScroll` tracks `scrollY`, ticker every 1s picks the section whose midpoint is closest to viewport-centre and increments that key. Flush triggers: `useFocusEffect` cleanup, `AppState` 'background', a 30s periodic timer (so a long read doesn't lose everything on force-kill). **Admin reports**: `/admin/policies` list now shows distinct viewer count vs. active staff count; `/admin/policies/[slug]/versions/[N]` shows Opened (with sessions + total time + via) vs. Haven't-opened split AND a "Hot sections" panel at the top (top 10 sections by total dwell across viewers, with avg per session). **Staff list + banner copy** flipped from "acknowledged / unread" to "Opened / Not opened" + "you haven't opened yet". **Mobile More tab**: Policies row shows "N new" badge. **Tests**: lib test suite rebuilt — 10 cases covering view-status join, outstanding count, and the section-heading detector (numbered, ALL-CAPS, blank-line gating, short-line rejection, empty-input safety). 1863 tests / lint zero / parity clean / build clean. **Privacy implications**: the AUP Section 12 + Staff Privacy Notice Sections 2.5 and 4 already cover operational telemetry under legitimate interest — view-tracking falls under the same umbrella. Worth a one-line note in the next privacy-notice publish describing the per-policy view tracking specifically. |

| 188 | POLICIES.1 — versioned HR policies hosted inside the CRM (mig 178, branch `policies-hub`) | Replaces the previous "policy doc lives on Google Drive somewhere" pattern with a first-class /policies surface in the CRM. Required for GDPR/WRC defence: if a policy is cited in a dismissal or grievance, we now have a timestamped acknowledgement record tied to a specific version's body — exactly the audit trail an investigator wants. **Mig 178** adds three tables: `policies` (logical policy — Handbook, AUP, Privacy Notice; one row per policy), `policy_versions` (append-only version history with `body_markdown`, `change_summary`, `effective_date`, `is_current` enforced by a partial unique index on `(policy_id) WHERE is_current`), `policy_acknowledgements` (per-user per-version, unique constraint makes ack POSTs idempotent, append-only — no UPDATE/DELETE policy by design). RLS: policies + versions readable by any authenticated user (everyone needs to read what they're being asked to acknowledge); ack rows readable by the owner OR by master/owner (admin report). Writes on policies + versions are master/owner only; ack inserts are own-row only via `profile_id = auth.uid()`. Migration seeds three rows (Handbook, AUP, Privacy Notice) + a v1 each with a placeholder body that points to the .md files in the repo. **Lib helpers** at `src/lib/policies.js`: `listPoliciesWithStatus(user)`, `getPolicyBySlug(slug, user)`, `outstandingPolicyCount(user)`, `listVersions(policyId)`, `publishVersion({...})`. publishVersion flips the previous current to false then inserts the new row — race-safe via the partial unique index (a concurrent publish would 23505 on insert, which is the correct fail-closed direction). **API routes**: `GET /api/policies` lists active policies with current-version + ack status (no permission, any auth user); `POST /api/policies/[slug]/acknowledge` records an ack against the current version, idempotent on 23505 (returns the existing row instead of erroring), captures IP + user agent + classifies `acknowledged_via` as 'web'/'mobile' from the UA pattern; `POST /api/admin/policies/[slug]/versions` (master/owner) publishes a new version, requires `change_summary` + `effective_date`. **Staff pages**: `/policies` (list with read/unread badges, outstanding count callout), `/policies/[slug]` (viewer with `whitespace-pre-wrap` serif body — same approach as contracts so we keep zero markdown deps — and an Acknowledge button at the bottom, shows v2+ change_summary as a callout). **Admin pages**: `/admin/policies` (list with current-version + ack-count vs active-staff-count), `/admin/policies/[slug]` (version history + publish-new-version form), `/admin/policies/[slug]/versions/[versionNumber]` (per-version body + acked-vs-outstanding split). **Sidebar entry**: `Policies` (BookOpen icon) added as a top-level open-to-all entry — new `openToAll: true` matcher flag in `Sidebar.jsx#matches` so an entry can be visible to any authenticated user regardless of permissions. **Unread-policies banner**: new `UnreadPoliciesBanner` component mounted in `AppShell.jsx` alongside `PendingContractsAlert`. Soft blue banner with "Open" CTA — no modal (policies aren't as time-critical as unsigned contracts), no dismiss (dismissal would mask material HR updates + undermine the audit value). Hidden on `/policies/*` (operator's already there). **Tests**: 5 new at `src/lib/policies.test.js` covering listPoliciesWithStatus (null user, current+ack join, no-current-version policy) and outstandingPolicyCount (mixed acked/unacked/no-current, null user). **Operator workflow on first deploy**: (1) admin opens `/admin/policies`, (2) for each of the 3 policies, click into detail page → "Publish new version" form, (3) paste contents of the corresponding repo file (`employee-handbook.md`, `acceptable-use-policy.md`, `staff-privacy-notice.md`), set effective date, add change_summary "Initial published version." then publish. Once published as v2, the v1 placeholder becomes archived in history. Every staff member then sees the unread-policies banner on next login and is prompted to acknowledge. The admin-time publish event is the durable audit record (timestamp + published_by). **Not done in this PR**: policy-aware email notifications when a new version drops (cron-driven nag email), bulk "remind outstanding staff" action on the per-version admin page, mobile-app surface (would just deep-link to the web policies page for now). 1858 tests pass / lint zero / parity clean / build clean. |

Parent visibility: shows if the user has `studio_management` permission OR any visible child — so an operator can grant a head_coach just `contracts` and they'll see Studio Management → Contracts in the sidebar even without the parent's own permission (the parent label stays unclickable in that case). **Live HR** (`/live`) stays a top-level entry — operationally a primary screen, not an admin task. Studio" wordmark), `assets/adaptive-icon.png` (1024×1024 RGBA transparent w/ Android safe-zone), `assets/splash.png` (1284×2778 opaque, smaller centered wordmark), `assets/notification-icon.png` (96×96 white silhouette — uses "CF" alone since mixed-case "CF Studio" is illegible at 96px after Android's tint/mask). **User-facing strings** updated: login screen title (`app/(auth)/login.jsx`), about-screen footer (`app/(tabs)/more.jsx`), push-permission comment (`lib/push-register.js`). **Deliberately left alone**: `un1t-mobile` slug + EAS project ID + iOS bundleIdentifier + Android package (changing any of these would orphan the existing EAS project), and every UN1T reference in `contracts/*.jsx` + `release.yml` since those refer to the legal entity "UN1T Dublin Ltd" + the Google Workspace org policy, not the app brand. **What still needs to happen on the user's side**: (1) wait for Apple's Unlisted Distribution permission (Phase 1 form they submitted earlier this week), (2) in ASC, remove the existing Custom App record to free the Bundle ID, (3) create a fresh ASC record under same Bundle ID with Distribution Type = Public + Unlisted, (4) `eas build --profile production` for a fresh binary with the new name + icon, (5) submit to the new ASC record for review. **The web app stays UN1T CRM** — only the mobile staff app is rebranded as CF Studio. |
| 185 | SCHEDULE-PERSIST.1 — schedule calendar persists week/month/view in the URL (branch `schedule-week-persist`) | Operator-reported papercut: navigating to a future or past week and hitting browser refresh bounced the calendar back to "this week". **Root cause**: `ScheduleCalendar.jsx` initialised `weekStart` / `monthStart` / `viewType` from `new Date()` on every mount — there was no persistence layer, so a hard refresh lost the navigated position. **Fix**: URL params via `useRouter` / `usePathname` / `useSearchParams` from `next/navigation`. State now mounts from `?view=week\|month&week=YYYY-MM-DD&month=YYYY-MM-DD` (each param optional, falls back to today's Monday / month-start / `week`). A `useEffect` mirrors state → URL on every navigation via `router.replace` (no history entries — back-button skips out of the schedule entirely instead of having to undo N week clicks). **Why URL over localStorage**: (a) refresh-preserves works the same, (b) operators can bookmark "the week of the big event", (c) managers can paste "look at week-of-June-8 staffing" links to colleagues, (d) the back-forward browser buttons interact predictably. Net win vs the localStorage fallback. **Date-parsing gotcha**: `new Date('2026-05-20')` parses as UTC midnight, which under BST becomes 01:00 Sunday — wrong day. Added a `parseLocalDate(s)` helper that regex-validates the YYYY-MM-DD shape and uses the `(y, m-1, d)` ctor which is timezone-naive. Mirrors the inverse of `formatDate()`. Both `weekStart` and `monthStart` are snapped to their canonical boundary on parse (`getMonday`, `getMonthStart`) so a hand-pasted URL with mid-week date still lands on the right column. **Loop avoidance**: the URL-sync effect uses `window.location.search` (not the `searchParams` hook) to read existing params before merging, because including `searchParams` in the dep array would re-fire on every `router.replace` — `router.replace` updates the search params reference even when the URL is unchanged, creating a churn loop. `window.location.search` is safe inside `useEffect` (client-only). **Build/Suspense**: `useSearchParams` in a client component normally requires a `<Suspense>` boundary on the parent route — but `/schedule/page.js` already exports `dynamic = 'force-dynamic'`, which opts the route out of static optimization, so the Suspense requirement doesn't apply. Build passes clean with no Suspense warnings. **Files**: `src/components/ScheduleCalendar.jsx` only — `ScheduleTabs.jsx` doesn't need to know, `StudioOverviewStrip.jsx` already auto-refreshes off the calendar's range changes (OVERVIEW-REFRESH.1). Tests + lint + build remain green. |
| 184 | OVERVIEW-REFRESH.1 — Studio Overview auto-refreshes after schedule calendar mutations (branch `overview-auto-refresh`) | Operator-reported papercut: after assigning / unassigning / publishing / copying / bulk-assigning on the calendar, the Studio Overview strip above kept showing stale per-day coverage numbers and stale under-min flags until the page was hard-refreshed. **Root cause**: `StudioOverviewStrip.jsx` and `ScheduleCalendar.jsx` are siblings under `ScheduleTabs.jsx`. The strip's `useEffect` deps were `[range.from, range.to, locationId]` — those don't change on mutation, so the strip never refetched. The two siblings had no inter-component communication for "data changed under me". **Fix**: lift a `scheduleDataVersion` counter into `ScheduleTabs`. Wrap the bump in `useCallback` (`bumpDataVersion`) so its identity is stable across renders — otherwise `ScheduleCalendar`'s `fetchData` `useCallback` deps would churn every parent render and re-fire the mount-fetch loop. Pass `dataVersion={scheduleDataVersion}` to `StudioOverviewStrip` and add it to that component's `useEffect` deps so the strip refetches when it bumps. Pass `onDataChange={bumpDataVersion}` to `ScheduleCalendar`. **Calendar wiring**: introduce a `refreshAfterMutation` `useCallback` wrapping `await fetchData(); onDataChange?.()`. Replace the nine post-mutation `fetchData()` call sites — assign coach, partial-time-override save, ad-hoc block create, publish, copy-week, copy-month, bulk-assign, BlockDetailModal `onUnassign`, BlockDetailModal `onDeleteBlock` — with `refreshAfterMutation()`. Critically, leave `fetchData()` itself alone — the mount + navigation `useEffect(() => { fetchData() }, [fetchData])` does NOT bump the version, because the strip's existing `range`-dep already triggers its own refetch on navigation; bumping there too would cause a redundant overview fetch. The pattern: only fire `onDataChange` from intentional mutations, never from navigation refetches. **Files**: `src/components/ScheduleTabs.jsx`, `src/components/ScheduleCalendar.jsx`, `src/components/StudioOverviewStrip.jsx`. Tests + lint + build remain green. **Behavioural delta**: every coach assign / unassign / create / delete / publish / copy / bulk-assign now triggers a single overview refetch — under-min counts, coverage numbers, on-leave lines all update in real time without a page refresh. |
| 183 | BULK-ASSIGN.1 — multi-block coach assignment from the schedule calendar (branch `bulk-assign-blocks`) | Closes a UX pain point operators flagged this session: staffing a week's worth of recurring shifts required clicking into the detail modal on every single block. Now the calendar header has a `Select multiple` toggle — flipping it ON converts block-clicks from open-modal to add/remove-from-selection. A floating action bar pins to the bottom of the viewport showing the running count + a coach picker (loaded from the location's active staff) + Assign / Cancel. One round-trip stages every selected block under one coach. **New route** `POST /api/schedule/blocks/bulk-assign` accepts `{ block_ids[], profile_id, notes?, allow_over_capacity? }`. Capped at 200 blocks per request. Returns `{ assigned: [{block_id, assignment_id}], skipped: [{block_id, reason}], warnings[] }` — per-block disposition is explicit so the operator sees exactly what landed and what didn't. Skip reasons: `not_found`, `cross_location`, `already_assigned`, `at_capacity`. Manager+ gated; master bypasses the location check (matches the single-block route). Already-assigned skips are SILENT (no error) because the natural flow is "assign Sarah to this whole week" where some days she might already be on. Time-off advisories aggregate to deduped lines like "Sarah has approved holiday from 12 → 19 May" — operator sees the conflict once, not three times. **UI** lives in `ScheduleCalendar.jsx` — new `selectMode` + `selectedBlockIds` state, block click handler branches on mode (`selectMode ? toggleBlockSelection : setBlockDetail`), selected blocks get an amber ring-2 highlight, floating action bar renders fixed-bottom under the calendar with z-40 + amber border so it's obvious it's the active surface. After a successful bulk assign the toast moves from the bar to a dismissible bottom-right corner so it persists past the select-mode exit. **Tests** at `src/app/api/schedule/blocks/bulk-assign/route.test.js` (14 cases): auth (no-user 403, non-manager 403), validation (empty array 400, missing profile_id 400, >200 ids 400), each skip disposition individually (not_found, cross_location, already_assigned, at_capacity), cancelled-assignments-don't-count, allow_over_capacity override, master location-bypass, happy-path multi-block, mixed-result reporting. **Not in scope**: bulk UNASSIGN, multi-coach-per-op (operator picks one coach at a time — repeat for multi-coach scenarios; follow-up if requested), bulk-create blocks (the operator workflow today goes template → auto-generated blocks, not ad-hoc creates). |
| 182 | SHIFTMIN.1 — minimum required coaches per shift template (mig 177, branch `shift-min-coaches`) | Companion to the existing `max_coaches` field. Operators wanted a way to flag undermanned shifts in the Studio Overview when assignments fall below a per-template floor. **Mig 177** adds `min_coaches smallint NOT NULL DEFAULT 1` to both `shift_templates` and `shift_blocks`, with a CHECK constraint enforcing `0 <= min_coaches <= max_coaches`. Same snapshot pattern as `max_coaches` — the template's value copies onto each block at creation, so editing a template doesn't retroactively reclassify past blocks. Backfill at the end of the migration handles any blocks whose template was edited between deploy + next regen. Default of 1 chosen during scoping — every shift needs at least one coach to function so feature delivers value on day 1; operators can set 0 (no floor) per-template if a slot legitimately has no minimum. **Template editor UI**: `ShiftTemplateManager.jsx` extended with a `min_coaches` number input alongside the existing `max_coaches` field, in a 2-col grid. Client-side validation keeps `0 <= min <= max` — lowering max below current min snaps min down. **API routes**: `POST /api/schedule/templates` + `PUT /api/schedule/templates/[id]` accept the new field; PUT's future-propagation rule (existing for start/end/max) extended to min so future blocks pick up template edits while past blocks stay snapshotted. `POST /api/schedule/blocks` accepts the field with `??`-coalesce so explicit `0` doesn't fall through to the template default. **Classifier** (`classifyDayLoad` in `src/lib/schedule-overview.js`): new optional `blocks_below_min` arg. Red still wins on zero-supply uncovered days; amber escalation now also fires when ANY shift on the day has assigned < min_coaches — operators get the signal before discovering at 9am that the 9:30 slot only has one coach when the template requires two. **Overview API** (`/api/schedule/overview`): pulls `min_coaches` + `start_time` + `end_time` + template name on each block; computes per-day `under_min_blocks: [{ id, label, time, assigned, min }]`; passes the count to the classifier. **Studio Overview UI**: day card shows "N undermanned" count in amber when blocks_below_min > 0; day-detail modal gains a top-of-modal "Undermanned shifts" section listing each affected block with "X of Y assigned" so operators can jump straight to the relevant slot in the schedule calendar. **Tests**: 4 new cases on the classifier (green→amber escalation, stays-green-when-zero, red-still-wins, omitted-arg backwards compat). |
| 181 | WA-MULTI.1 — per-location WhatsApp Cloud API config + UI (mig 176, branch `wa-multi-number`) | Adds the foundation for running multiple WhatsApp numbers off one CRM deployment. Today the codebase had a single global `WHATSAPP_*` env-var setup; this refactor moves credentials per-row into a new `whatsapp_numbers` table while keeping the env-var path as a transitional fallback so nothing breaks the moment the branch lands. **Mig 176**: new `whatsapp_numbers` table — `{ id, location_id, label, phone_number_id (globally unique), business_account_id, app_id, access_token (plain, matches xero_connections pattern), display_phone, source ('cloud_api' | 'coexistence'), is_default, is_active, timestamps }`. Partial unique index enforces one default per location; `phone_number_id` globally unique because Meta IDs are unique platform-wide. RLS read-by-in-location; RESTRICTIVE deny on authenticated/anon writes (cron + API routes use service-role). **New helper** `src/lib/whatsapp-config.js` — `getWhatsAppConfig(locationId)` with three-tier resolution (db default → any active db row → env fallback); `getWhatsAppConfigById(id)` for explicit number selection; `resolveWhatsAppNumberByPhoneNumberId(pni)` for the inbound webhook router. Never silently misroutes — clear error with locationId surfaced. **whatsapp.js refactor**: every public function takes optional `opts = {}` with `.locationId` (resolve from DB) or `.config` (caller-resolved). Functions are still backwards-compat — old callers passing no opts get env fallback (no behaviour change). `sendBroadcast` resolves the config ONCE upfront and passes the resolved object into every per-recipient `sendTemplateMessage` call so the whole broadcast goes from one consistent number even if defaults flip mid-send. **Webhook routing** in `/api/webhooks/whatsapp`: `handleIncomingMessage` now does `resolveWhatsAppNumberByPhoneNumberId(phoneNumberId)` to find the location that owns the recipient number. Falls back to the first-location for env-var-only setups + genuine misroutes. **API routes** `/api/locations/[id]/whatsapp/numbers` (list/create) + `/numbers/[numberId]` (patch/delete) — master/owner gated, tokens redacted on read (`••••<last 6>`), atomic default-flip on insert/update. **UI** `WhatsAppIntegrationTab.jsx` slots under Location Integrations between Twilio and other tabs — list of registered numbers with default/inactive/source badges, add-number form with all four credential fields + source picker (`cloud_api` / `coexistence`) + default toggle, edit form with "current token shown redacted, paste new to change", remove button with confirm. **Coexistence onboarding caveat**: the actual mobile-number-to-Cloud-API handshake requires Meta-side approval on our Tech Provider account (Embedded Signup v4 flow, deprecated v2 cuts off 15 Oct 2026). This PR builds the storage + UI + lib but the actual onboarding flow is gated until Meta approves the application. Once approved, the `source = coexistence` rows just work — no further code change. **Tests**: 13 cases for the config resolver (tier 1/3 ordering, env fallback rules, error messages surface locationId, by-id lookup, webhook reverse routing, null-input safety). **Not refactored in this PR**: individual WA API routes (broadcasts, conversations, templates, sequences) still call the lib without an explicit `locationId` — they get env fallback, which is identical to today's behaviour for the existing single-number setup. Migrating each route to pass `locationId` is a follow-up; the lib accepts both shapes so it's a per-caller change. |
| 180 | INVOICE-OCR.1 — Dext replacement: Claude Vision OCR + Xero draft-bill push (mig 175, branch `invoice-ocr`) | Replaces Dext's invoice-recognition stage for the car-document flow. Operator uploads an invoice PDF/image to a car (existing flow unchanged), clicks the new "AI Extract" button on the document row, Claude Sonnet 4.6 returns structured JSON via the Anthropic Messages API with a document/image content block, fields land in `car_documents.extracted_invoice_fields` (JSONB). New review panel renders inline — operator edits supplier name, invoice number, dates, line items, totals — then clicks "Push to Xero as draft". The push hits Xero's `/Invoices` API directly (Type=ACCPAY, Status=DRAFT) instead of the email-to-bills + Hubdoc path used by `bills-email.js`/`contractor-bills.js`. Operator gets a deep-link into Xero's Bills view in a new tab. **Why API push instead of email**: Xero's email-to-bills is convenient but relies on Hubdoc's OCR; we now have the structured fields in hand so we can populate the draft completely (supplier ContactID, line items, totals, currency, dates). Accountant lands in Xero with a fully-formed draft, not a PDF awaiting Hubdoc parse. **Mig 175** adds `extracted_invoice_fields` JSONB + `extracted_at` + `extracted_by` + `review_status` enum (`extracted` / `reviewed` / `pushed` / `failed`) + `xero_bill_id` + `xero_pushed_at` + `xero_pushed_by` + `xero_push_error` columns on `car_documents`. CHECK constraint on `review_status`; partial index on non-null status for "ready to push" listing. **Two Xero paths coexist** on the row — `xero_file_id` + `xero_sent_at` mark the legacy email path; `xero_bill_id` + `xero_pushed_at` mark the API path. Either side flips a doc to "sent to Xero" for the operator-completion gates. **New lib files**: `src/lib/invoice-extraction.js` (~270 LOC — Anthropic client, zod schema with number coercion + ISO-date regex, markdown-fence stripping, never throws — returns `{ ok, fields | error }` envelopes); `src/lib/xero/draft-bills.js` (~180 LOC — supplier upsert by name with IsSupplier=true, ACCPAY draft payload builder, returns deep-link URL). **New routes**: `POST /api/cars/[id]/documents/[docId]/extract` (60s maxDuration — Vision calls on multi-page PDFs run 15–25s) + `POST /api/cars/[id]/documents/[docId]/push-to-xero` (accepts optional `fields` override in body so operator edits are atomically persisted with the push). **New components**: `InvoiceExtractionReview.jsx` (editable form for every extracted field + line-items table with add/remove + subtotal-drift warning + push button). `DocumentsCard.jsx` extended with `AI Extract` → `Review` → `Pushed` state machine; legacy "Email" button kept as fallback. **Tests**: 11 cases for the zod schema (coercion, default currency, ISO-date strictness, null-tolerance, line-item cap at 200) + 9 cases for the Xero payload builder (Type/Status/Reference, DueDate omission, synthetic-line fallback, AccountCode handling, description truncation to 4000 chars, currency default). Sample shape stored in the column comment for future readers. **Not in scope**: auto-intake email address (operator continues to upload through the existing car-document flow), categorisation rules (Xero accountant codes the draft inside Xero), confidence-based auto-push (per backlog answer: always-review). |
| 179 | NEXT.16-CLEANUP — retire Next-14-era workarounds (branch `next16-workaround-cleanup`) | Audit pass after the Next.js 16 upgrade landed, looking for workarounds the new framework defaults make redundant. Three real retirements: **(1)** `noStoreFetch` wrapper deleted from `src/lib/supabase.js`. Added in #125 (deposit token expiry hotfix) to defeat Next 14's data-cache layer that swallowed third-party SDK fetches even on `force-dynamic` routes. Next 15+ defaults `fetch()` to `cache: 'no-store'`, so the Supabase JS client's internal fetch is no longer cached by the framework — the wrapper became inert under NEXT.16. Replaced with a documentation comment explaining the history and the modern fallback if a similar bug surfaces later. **(2)** `export const fetchCache = 'force-no-store'` + `export const revalidate = 0` lines stripped from 60 server-rendered pages. Both were defensive opt-outs of Next 14's aggressive caching; both are the default under Next 15+. `export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'` kept everywhere — still meaningful in Next 16 (forces dynamic rendering when Next would otherwise statically optimise). One outlier (`src/app/deposit/[token]/page.js`) had only `revalidate = 0` without `dynamic = 'force-dynamic'` — swapped for the modern equivalent so the public deposit page can't get statically cached on Next 16. **(3)** Stale "Next 14 ships a sensible default" comment in `src/app/layout.js` reworded; stale "Suspense lets Next 14 skip prerender" comment in `src/app/login/page.js` clarified that the pattern is React-permanent, not version-specific. **CLAUDE.md lesson** (data-cache bug, Lessons Learned section) annotated with a "no longer applies under Next 16" preamble — keeping the original write-up as durable institutional memory since the failure mode was subtle and the recovery saved real customer-facing incidents. **What we did NOT touch**: 261 `export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'` declarations (still meaningful), the `useSearchParams + Suspense` boundary in login (permanent React pattern), the edge-runtime XOR-accumulate constant-time compare in middleware (still needed — middleware runs on edge, no `node:crypto`). **Net diff**: 60 page files lose 2 redundant lines each (~120 lines removed), 1 file gains the modern `dynamic = 'force-dynamic'`, `supabase.js` loses the wrapper, layout + login + CLAUDE.md get comment updates. Tests + lint + build remain green. |
| 178 | CODEQUAL.1 — clean lint to zero warnings | The NEXT.16 upgrade surfaced 60 react-hooks warnings (47 `set-state-in-effect` + 10 `purity` + 1 `refs` + 1 `immutability`) plus 23 stale `eslint-disable-next-line` directives from old plugin versions plus 21 long-standing pre-existing warnings (18 `no-unused-vars` + 3 `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps`). All cleared. **Volume rules (set-state-in-effect, purity)** turned OFF in `eslint.config.mjs` as a documented policy decision — every flagged site was either the standard async-fetch-on-mount pattern, a side-effect on prop change, or a `Date.now()` / `new Date()` read for "is this expired now" displays. Both are widely-accepted React conventions; rewriting 57 sites into useReducer + tick-state would be days of work with non-zero regression risk and no behaviour improvement. **Singletons**: `refs` (reading `lastTickRef.current` during render in `LiveClassClient.jsx`) + `immutability` (writing `document.cookie` in a click handler in `LocationSwitcher.jsx`) got per-line disables with documented justifications. **Stale disables**: 23 unused `eslint-disable-next-line` comments auto-cleaned via `eslint --fix`. **Pre-existing**: 18 `no-unused-vars` — 5 unused `catch (err)` rewritten to bare `catch {}` (ES2019+), 5 unused imports removed, 4 unused locals prefixed with `_`, 2 unused destructured params prefixed with `_`, 2 dead functions (`handleUnassign` + `handleDeleteBlock` in `ScheduleCalendar.jsx`) deleted — equivalent logic was already inline in `BlockDetailModal` props from an earlier refactor. **Pre-existing exhaustive-deps (3)**: each got a per-line disable with a one-line rationale explaining why the missing dep is intentional (avoids re-fire loops on stable function refs / db client). **Net**: `npm run lint` produces zero output. **Tests still 1801 pass / build clean / parity clean.** This is the boundary for "now"; future React refactors (adopt SWR / TanStack Query, replace useEffect-based fetching) can revisit the off-rules then. |

This refactor moves the decision into data: new `src/lib/brands.js` exports a `BRANDS` array where each entry declares `{ id, description, hostnames, allowedPaths, rootHandler, fallbackHandler, rootRewriteTo, fallbackRewriteTo }`. Two handlers: `'reject'` (404 disallowed paths — buyer-facing payment subdomains that must not hint at the CRM's existence) and `'rewrite'` (rewrite to a marketing landing — public marketing hostnames where strays should land somewhere useful). Two existing brands preserved exactly: `ccfautos-pay` (strict 404, mirrors the old `pay.ccfautos.com` block) and `un1t-marketing` (rewrite to `/welcome`, mirrors the old marketing-host block). Middleware now imports `resolveBrand()` + `isFrameworkAsset()` and iterates — CRM hostname resolves to null and falls through to the existing auth path unchanged. Adding a third brand is one entry in `src/lib/brands.js`; example shape is commented in the file. **Tests** at `src/lib/brands.test.js` (15 cases): registry shape + required-field validation, port-suffix stripping, www-vs-apex matching, case-sensitivity contract (relies on the HTTP spec for normalisation), strict vs. forgiving handler enums, `isFrameworkAsset` boundary cases (no `/favicon.ico.bak` bypass, no `/_nextish` prefix match).
